### Q1. JEE Main 2026 (06 April Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A current of $30$ A each flows in opposite directions in two conducting wires, placed parallel to each other at a distance of $8$ cm. The magnetic field at the mid point between the two wires is __________ $\mu$T. $\left(\dfrac{\mu_0}{4\pi} = 10^{-7}\text{ N/A}^2\right)$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $30$
- **(B)** $300$
- **(C)** $150$
- **(D)** $0.0$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $300$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Let the distance between the wires be $d = 8\text{ cm}$. The distance of the midpoint from each wire is $r = \dfrac{d}{2} = 4\text{ cm} = 0.04\text{ m}$.

The current in each wire is $I = 30\text{ A}$.

Since the currents flow in opposite directions, the magnetic fields produced by both wires at the midpoint point in the same direction and add up.

The total magnetic field at the midpoint is:

$B_{\text{net}} = B_1 + B_2 = \dfrac{\mu_0 I}{2\pi r} + \dfrac{\mu_0 I}{2\pi r} = \dfrac{\mu_0 I}{\pi r}$

Substituting the given values:

$B_{\text{net}} = \dfrac{4\pi \times 10^{-7} \times 30}{\pi \times 0.04}$

$B_{\text{net}} = \dfrac{120 \times 10^{-7}}{0.04}$

$B_{\text{net}} = 3000 \times 10^{-7}\text{ T}$

$B_{\text{net}} = 300 \times 10^{-6}\text{ T} = 300\ \mu\text{T}$

Answer: $300$

</details>

### Q2. JEE Main 2026 (06 April Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A small cube of side $1$ mm is placed at the centre of a circular loop of radius $10$ cm carrying a current of $2$ A. The magnetic energy stored inside the cube is $\alpha \times 10^{-14}$ J. The value of $\alpha$ is _______. ($\mu_o = 4\pi \times 10^{-7}$ Tm/A, $\pi = 3.14$)

**Options:**

- **(A)** $6.28$
- **(B)** $6.28 \times 10^{-6}$
- **(C)** $628$
- **(D)** $6.28 \times 10^{-4}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $6.28$**

**Detailed Solution:**

The magnetic field at the centre of the circular loop is given by $B = \dfrac{\mu_0 I}{2R}$

Substituting the given values, $B = \dfrac{4\pi \times 10^{-7} \times 2}{2 \times 0.1} = 4\pi \times 10^{-6}$ T

The magnetic energy density is $u = \dfrac{B^2}{2\mu_0}$

$u = \dfrac{(4\pi \times 10^{-6})^2}{2 \times 4\pi \times 10^{-7}} = \dfrac{16\pi^2 \times 10^{-12}}{8\pi \times 10^{-7}} = 2\pi \times 10^{-5}$ J/m$^3$

The volume of the small cube is $V = (1 \text{ mm})^3 = (10^{-3} \text{ m})^3 = 10^{-9}$ m$^3$

The magnetic energy stored inside the cube is $U = u \times V$

$U = 2\pi \times 10^{-5} \times 10^{-9} = 2\pi \times 10^{-14}$ J

Using $\pi = 3.14$, we get $U = 2 \times 3.14 \times 10^{-14} = 6.28 \times 10^{-14}$ J

Comparing with $\alpha \times 10^{-14}$ J, we get $\alpha = 6.28$

Answer: $6.28$

</details>

### Q3. JEE Main 2026 (28 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A long cylindrical conductor with large cross section carries an electric current distributed uniformly over its cross-section. Magnetic field due to this current is : 
 A. maximum at either ends of the conductor and minimum at the midpoint 
 B. maximum at the axis of the conductor 
 C. minimum at the surface of the conductor 
 D. minimum at the axis of the conductor 
 E. same at all points in the cross-section of the conductor 
 Choose the correct answer from the options given below :

**Options:**

- **(A)** A, D Only
- **(B)** D Only
- **(C)** E Only
- **(D)** B, C Only

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) D Only**

**Detailed Solution:**

For a long cylindrical conductor with uniform current distribution, apply Ampere's law to find the magnetic field.
At distance $r$ from the axis (inside the conductor, $r < R$): $B(r) = \frac{\mu_0 I r}{2\pi R^2}$ (proportional to $r$)
At the axis ($r = 0$): $B = 0$ (minimum)
At the surface ($r = R$): $B = \frac{\mu_0 I}{2\pi R}$ (maximum)
Analyzing the statements:
A. Maximum at ends and minimum at midpoint - FALSE. This describes behavior along the axis, but the magnetic field depends on radial distance, not axial position.
B. Maximum at the axis of the conductor - FALSE. The field is zero at the axis.
C. Minimum at the surface of the conductor - FALSE. The field is maximum at the surface.
D. Minimum at the axis of the conductor - TRUE. The field is zero at $r = 0$, making it the minimum.
E. Same at all points in the cross-section - FALSE. The field varies from 0 at the axis to maximum at the surface.
Only D is correct.

</details>

### Q4. JEE Main 2026 (28 January Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The magnetic field at the centre of a current carrying circular loop of radius $R$ is $16 \mu \mathrm{~T}$. The magnetic field at a distance $x=\sqrt{3} R$ on its axis from the centre is $\_\_\_\_$ $\mu \mathrm{T}$.

**Options:**

- **(A)** 4
- **(B)** 2
- **(C)** $2 \sqrt{2}$
- **(D)** 8

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) 2**

**Detailed Solution:**

The magnetic field at the center of a circular loop is $B_{center} = \frac{\mu_0 I}{2R} = 16\mu$ T.
On the axis at distance $x$ from center: $B(x) = \frac{\mu_0 I R^2}{2(R^2 + x^2)^{3/2}}$.
At $x = \sqrt{3}R$: $B(\sqrt{3}R) = \frac{\mu_0 I R^2}{2(R^2 + 3R^2)^{3/2}} = \frac{\mu_0 I R^2}{2(4R^2)^{3/2}} = \frac{\mu_0 I}{16R}$.
The ratio: $\frac{B(\sqrt{3}R)}{B_{center}} = \frac{1/16R}{1/2R} = \frac{1}{8}$.
Therefore: $B(\sqrt{3}R) = \frac{1}{8} \times 16\mu = 2\mu$ T.

</details>

### Q5. JEE Main 2026 (24 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

<img style="width:300px;" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_watermark_improved/24S2_q_32_1.png"> 
 Two identical circular loops $P$ and $Q$ each of radius $r$ are lying in parallel planes such that they have common axis. The current through $P$ and $Q$ are $I$ and $4 I$ respectively in clockwise direction as seen from $O$. The net magnetic field at $O$ is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{3 \mu_{\mathrm{o}} I}{4 \sqrt{2} r}$ towards $Q$
- **(B)** $\frac{\mu_{\mathrm{o}} I}{4 \sqrt{2} r}$ towards $Q$
- **(C)** $\frac{3 \mu_{0} I}{4 \sqrt{2} r}$ towards $P$
- **(D)** $\frac{\mu_{\mathrm{o}} I}{4 \sqrt{2} r}$ towards $P$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\frac{3 \mu_{\mathrm{o}} I}{4 \sqrt{2} r}$ towards $Q$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Two identical circular loops P and Q, each of radius $r$, are separated by distance $r$ along their common axis. Current in P is $I$ and in Q is $4I$ (both clockwise as seen from O).
Point O is at the midpoint. The magnetic fields from each loop at O:
$B_1 = \frac{\mu_0 I R^2}{2(R^2 + R^2)^{3/2}}$ (from P, pointing toward Q)
$B_2 = \frac{4\mu_0 I R^2}{2(R^2 + R^2)^{3/2}}$ (from Q, pointing toward P)
$B_{net} = B_2 - B_1 = \frac{3\mu_0 I R^2}{2(2R^2)^{3/2}} = \frac{3\mu_0 I}{4\sqrt{2}R}$
Direction: towards Q.

</details>

### Q6. JEE Main 2026 (23 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The current passing through a conducting loop in the form of equilateral triangle of side $4 \sqrt{3} \mathrm{~cm}$ is 2 A. The magnetic field at its centroid is $\alpha \times 10^{-5} \mathrm{~T}$. The value of $\alpha$ is $\_\_\_\_$. 
 (Given : $\mu_{\mathrm{o}}=4 \pi \times 10^{-7}$ SI units)

**Options:**

- **(A)** $2 \sqrt{3}$
- **(B)** $\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}$
- **(C)** $3 \sqrt{3}$
- **(D)** $\sqrt{3}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $3 \sqrt{3}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For an equilateral triangle of side $a$, the distance of the centroid from any side is $r = \frac{a}{2\sqrt{3}}$.
Given side $a = 4\sqrt{3}$ cm $= 4\sqrt{3} \times 10^{-2}$ m.
So, $r = \frac{4\sqrt{3} \times 10^{-2}}{2\sqrt{3}} = 2 \times 10^{-2}$ m.
The magnetic field at the centroid due to one side of the triangle is $B_1 = \frac{\mu_0 I}{4\pi r} (\sin \theta_1 + \sin \theta_2)$.
For an equilateral triangle, $\theta_1 = \theta_2 = 60^\circ$.
$B_1 = \frac{10^{-7} \times 2}{2 \times 10^{-2}} (\sin 60^\circ + \sin 60^\circ) = 10^{-5} \times (\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2} + \frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}) = \sqrt{3} \times 10^{-5}$ T.
The total magnetic field at the centroid due to all three sides is $B = 3B_1 = 3\sqrt{3} \times 10^{-5}$ T.
Comparing with $B = \alpha \times 10^{-5}$ T, we get $\alpha = 3\sqrt{3}$.

</details>

### Q7. JEE Main 2026 (21 January Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

An infinitely long straight wire carrying current $I$ is bent in a planer shape as shown in the diagram. The radius of the circular part is $r$. The magnetic field at the centre $O$ of the circular loop is : 
<img style="width:300px;" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_watermark_improved/21S2_q_35_1.png">

**Options:**

- **(A)** $-\frac{\mu_{0}}{2 \pi} \frac{I}{r}(\pi-1) \hat{i}$
- **(B)** $\frac{\mu_{0}}{2 \pi} \frac{I}{r}(\pi-1) \hat{i}$
- **(C)** $-\frac{\mu_{0}}{2 \pi} \frac{I}{r}(\pi+1) \hat{i}$
- **(D)** $\frac{\mu_{0}}{2 \pi} \frac{I}{r}(\pi+1) \hat{i}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $-\frac{\mu_{0}}{2 \pi} \frac{I}{r}(\pi-1) \hat{i}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img style="width:400px;" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main_2026_watermark_improved/21S2_s_35.png">
$\overrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}_0=\overrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathrm{AB}}+\overrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathrm{DE}}+\overrightarrow{\mathrm{B}}_{\mathrm{BCD}}$
$=\frac{\mu_0 \mathrm{i}}{4 \pi \mathrm{r}} \hat{\mathrm{i}}+\frac{\mu_0 \mathrm{i}}{4 \pi \mathrm{r}} \hat{\mathrm{i}}-\frac{\mu_0 \mathrm{i}}{2 \mathrm{r}} \hat{\mathrm{i}}$
$=\frac{\mu_0 \mathrm{i}}{2 \pi \mathrm{r}} \hat{\mathrm{i}}-\frac{\mu_0 \mathrm{i}}{2 \mathrm{r}} \hat{\mathrm{i}}$
$=\frac{\mu_0 \mathrm{i}}{2 \pi \mathrm{r}}(1-\pi) \hat{\mathrm{i}}$
$=-\frac{\mu_0 \mathrm{i}}{2 \pi \mathrm{r}}(\pi-1) \hat{\mathrm{i}}$

</details>

Figure shows a current carrying square loop ABCD of edge length is ' $a$ ' lying in a plane. If the resistance of the $A B C$ part is $r$ and that of $A D C$ part is 2 r , then the magnitude of the resultant magnetic field at centre of the square loop is
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/Vr8JuWaeNVl8ZKSAIiXsQ2Js2ypjlHbg6t4uYGE6qxM.original.fullsize.png"/>

- **(B)** $\frac{\mu_0 \mathrm{I}}{2 \pi \mathrm{a}}$
- **(C)** $\frac{\sqrt{2} \mu_0 \mathrm{I}}{3 \pi \mathrm{a}}$
- **(D)** $\frac{2 \mu_0 \mathrm{I}}{3 \pi \mathrm{a}}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/9wzWCy6E31UY5aGgvMxAac9ksiwRrUh20rlY5BnC2Zk.original.fullsize.png"/>

### Q9. JEE Main 2025 (3 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

A loop ABCDA , carrying current $\mathrm{I}=12 \mathrm{~A}$, is placed in a plane, consists of two semi-circular segments of radius $R_1=6 \pi \mathrm{~m}$ and $\mathrm{R}_2=4 \pi \mathrm{~m}$. The magnitude of the resultant magnetic field at center O is $\mathrm{k} \times 10^{-7} \mathrm{~T}$. The value of k is ______ (Given $\mu_0=4 \pi \times 10^{-7} \mathrm{Tm} \mathrm{A}^{-1}$)
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/XRrhnIYCmG0Pgcy2bZ-aziQ7-wabhiUkus3tEEryVhw.original.fullsize.png"/>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **1**

**Detailed Solution:**

Magnetic field due to $\mathrm{AB} \& \mathrm{CD}=0$
$\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{B}_0=\left|\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{R}_1}-\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{R}_2}\right| \\
& =\frac{\mu_0 \mathrm{I}}{4 \mathrm{R}_2}-\frac{\mu_0 \mathrm{I}}{4 \mathrm{R}_1} \\
& =\frac{4 \pi \times 10^{-7} \times 12}{4}\left(\frac{1}{4 \pi}-\frac{1}{6 \pi}\right) \\
& =12 \pi \times 10^{-7}\left(\frac{1}{12 \pi}\right) \\
& =1 \times 10^{-7}
\end{aligned}$
$K=1$

</details>

### Q10. JEE Main 2025 (2 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Let $B_1$ be the magnitude of magnetic field at center of a circular coil of radius $R$ carrying current $I$. Let $B_2$ be the magnitude of magnetic field at an axial distance ' $x$ ' from the center. For $x: R=3: 4, \frac{B_2}{B_1}$ is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $4: 5$
- **(B)** $16: 25$
- **(C)** $64: 125$
- **(D)** $25: 16$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $64: 125$**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/RRz7Q_4AJZM3z8mNQUJMtSembhHWyK9nSvaWONjGWW4.original.fullsize.png"/>
$\begin{aligned} & \mathrm{B}_1=\frac{\mu_0 \mathrm{i}}{2 \mathrm{R}} \qquad \mathrm{B}_2=\mathrm{B}_1 \sin ^3 \theta \\ & \therefore \frac{\mathrm{~B}_2}{\mathrm{~B}_1}=\sin ^3 \theta=\left(\frac{4}{5}\right)^3=\frac{64}{125}\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q11. JEE Main 2025 (29 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

The magnetic field inside a 200 turns solenoid of radius 10 cm is $2.9 \times 10^{-4} \mathrm{Tesla}$. If the solenoid carries a current of 0.29 A , then the length of the solenoid is ________ $\pi \mathrm{cm}$.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **8**

**Detailed Solution:**

Assuming long solenoid
$\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{B}=\mu_0\left(\frac{\mathrm{~N}}{\ell}\right) \mathrm{i} \\
& \ell=\frac{\mu_0 \mathrm{Ni}}{\mathrm{~B}}=\frac{\left(4 \pi \times 10^{-7}\right)(200)(0.29)}{2.9 \times 10^{-4}} \mathrm{~m} \\
& =8 \pi \mathrm{~cm}
\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q12. JEE Main 2025 (29 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Consider a long straight wire of a circular cross-section (radius a) carrying a steady current I. The current is uniformly distributed across this cross-section. The distances from the centre of the wire's cross-section at which the magnetic field [inside the wire, outside the wire] is half of the maximum possible magnetic field, any where due to the wire, will be

**Options:**

- **(A)** \([a / 4,3 a / 2]\)
- **(B)** \([a / 4,2 a]\)
- **(C)** \([\mathrm{a} / 2,2 \mathrm{a}]\)
- **(D)** \([a / 2,3 a]\)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) \([\mathrm{a} / 2,2 \mathrm{a}]\)**

**Detailed Solution:**

Maximum possible magnetic field is at the surface
$\begin{aligned}
& \mathrm{B}_{\max }=\frac{\mu_0 \mathrm{I}}{2 \pi \mathrm{a}} \\
& \frac{\mathrm{~B}_{\max }}{2}=\frac{\mu_0 \mathrm{I}}{4 \pi \mathrm{a}}
\end{aligned}$

It can be obtained inside as well as outside the wire For inside,
$\begin{aligned}
& \frac{\mu_0 \mathrm{I}}{4 \pi \mathrm{a}}=\frac{\mu_0 \mathrm{Ir}}{2 \pi \mathrm{a}^2} \\
& \Rightarrow \mathrm{r}=\frac{\mathrm{a}}{2}
\end{aligned}$

For outside
$\begin{aligned}
& \frac{\mu_0 \mathrm{I}}{4 \pi \mathrm{a}}=\frac{\mu_0 \mathrm{I}}{2 \pi \mathrm{r}} \\
& \Rightarrow \mathrm{r}=2 \mathrm{a}
\end{aligned}$

Correct answer $\left[\frac{\mathrm{a}}{2}, 2 \mathrm{a}\right]$

</details>

### Q15. JEE Main 2025 (24 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

A current of 5A exists in a square loop of side $\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}} \mathrm{~m}$. Then the magnitude of the magnetic field $B$ at the centre of the square loop will be $p \times 10^{-6} \mathrm{~T}$. where, value of p is _____ .
$\left[\right.$ Take $\left.\mu_0=4 \pi \times 10^{-7} \mathrm{TmA}^{-1}\right]$.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **8**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/pySAD5X6_LyAZco4PYXpIM73nvSRgqoU0bOZH0dYkSc.original.fullsize.png">




Let B be the magnetic field due to single side
$\begin{aligned}
& \text { then } B=\frac{\mu_0 \mathrm{i}}{4 \pi \mathrm{~d}}\left(\sin \theta_1+\sin \theta_2\right) \\
& =\frac{10^{-7} \times 5 \times 2}{\frac{1}{2 \sqrt{2}}} \times \frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}=2 \times 10^{-6} \\
& \therefore B_{\text {net }} \text { at centre } O=4 B \\
& =8 \times 10^{-6}
\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q16. JEE Main 2024 (08 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A long straight wire of radius a carries a steady current I. The current is uniformly distributed across its cross section. The ratio of the magnetic field at $\frac{a}{2}$ and $2 a$ from axis of the wire is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1: 4$
- **(B)** $1: 1$
- **(C)** $3: 4$
- **(D)** $4: 1$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $1: 1$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\begin{aligned} & \mathrm{B}_1 2 \pi \frac{\mathrm{a}}{2}=\mu_{\mathrm{o}} \frac{\mathrm{I}}{4} \\ & \mathrm{~B}_1=\frac{\mu_{\mathrm{o}} \mathrm{I}}{4 \pi \mathrm{a}} \\ & \mathrm{B}_2 2 \pi 2 \mathrm{a}=\mu_{\mathrm{o}} \mathrm{I} \\ & \mathrm{B}_2=\frac{\mu_{\mathrm{o}} \mathrm{I}}{4 \pi \mathrm{a}}\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q17. JEE Main 2024 (05 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `3`

A solenoid of length $0.5 \mathrm{~m}$ has a radius of $1 \mathrm{~cm}$ and is made up of ' $\mathrm{m}$ ' number of turns. It carries a current of $5 \mathrm{~A}$. If the magnitude of the magnetic field inside the solenoid is $6.28 \times 10^{-3} \mathrm{~T}$ then the value of $m$ is _____.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **500**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\mu_0 n i=B \quad n=$ number of turns per unit length
$\mu_0\left(\frac{\mathrm{m}}{\ell}\right) \mathrm{i}=\mathrm{B}$
$\begin{aligned} & \mathrm{m}=\frac{B . \ell}{\mu_0 \mathrm{i}}=\frac{6.28 \times 10^{-3} \times 0.5}{12.56 \times 10^{-7} \times 5} \\ & \mathrm{~m}=500\end{aligned}$

</details>

### Q18. JEE Main 2024 (05 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

In a co-axial straight cable, the central conductor and the outer conductor carry equal currents in opposite directions. The magnetic field is zero :

**Options:**

- **(A)** outside the cable
- **(B)** inside the outer conductor
- **(C)** inside the inner conductor
- **(D)** in between the two conductors

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) outside the cable**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/rgQ-_l-FCY5tB9Ph7lL-I7zzjn5UpOr3bhcWT35O8LU.original.fullsize.png">
$\oint \overrightarrow{\mathrm{B}} \cdot \mathrm{d} \vec{\ell}=\mu_0 \mathrm{i}_{\mathrm{enc}}=0$
$\therefore \mathrm{B}=0$ outside the cable

</details>

### Q19. JEE Main 2024 (01 Feb Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

A regular polygon of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn></math> sides is formed by bending a wire of length <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi></math> meter. If an electric current of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mi>π</mi><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> is flowing through the sides of the polygon, the magnetic field at the centre of the polygon would be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mo>.</mo></math> The value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math> is ______.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **72**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/172ddb07-d81f-4ecb-b61f-e325e9d6890a.png" style="width: 150px; height: 178px;"/></p><p>Direction of magnetic field due to all six sides will be in the same direction, therefore we can write </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>μ</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>π</mi><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mfenced><mrow><mi>sin</mi><mn>30</mn><mo>°</mo><mo>+</mo><mi>sin</mi><mn>30</mn><mo>°</mo></mrow></mfenced></math>, where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>r</mi><mo>=</mo></math>distance of the sides from the centre.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn><mfrac><mrow><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup><mo>×</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>π</mi><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></mrow><mfenced><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac><mi>π</mi><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>×</mo><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></mstyle></mfenced></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>72</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math></p><p>Hence, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>72</mn></math>.</p>

</details>

---

### Q20. JEE Main 2024 (31 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `3`

<p>Two circular coils <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi></math> of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>100</mn></math> turns each have same radius of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>π</mi><mo> </mo><mi>cm</mi></math>. The currents in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi></math> are <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> respectively. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Q</mi></math> are placed with their planes mutually perpendicular with their centers coincide. The resultant magnetic field induction at the center of the coils is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mi>x</mi></msqrt><mo> </mo><mi>mT</mi></math>, where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo></math> ______. [Use <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>μ</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>π</mi><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>−</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mrow><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>]</p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **20**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Consider two coils as shown below:</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/431b2baf-1955-4ea8-9002-781155348bcd.png" style="width: 350px; height: 170px;"/></p><p>Magnetic field due to P, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mi>P</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>N</mi><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>μ</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>i</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>100</mn><mo>×</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>μ</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>×</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mfenced><mrow><mi>π</mi><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>−</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math></p><p>Magnetic field due to Q, </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mi>Q</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>N</mi><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>μ</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>i</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>100</mn><mo>×</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>μ</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>×</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>−</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math></p><p>Therefore, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mi>net</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msqrt><msubsup><mi>B</mi><mi>P</mi><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>+</mo><msubsup><mi>B</mi><mi>Q</mi><mn>2</mn></msubsup></msqrt></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><msqrt><mn>20</mn><mo> </mo></msqrt><mo> </mo><mi>mT</mi></math></p><p>Hence, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>20</mn></math></p>

</details>

### Q21. JEE Main 2024 (30 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

The current of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> flows in a square loop of sides <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> is placed in air. The magnetic field at the centre of the loop is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>. The value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">X</mi></math> is _________.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **40**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Magnetic field at the centre of the square loop is given by,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>μ</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>π</mi><mi>d</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mi>sin</mi><mn>45</mn><mo>°</mo><mo>+</mo><mi>sin</mi><mn>45</mn><mo>°</mo></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfenced></math>, where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>d</mi></math> is perpendicular distance of the centre to one of its sides.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo mathvariant="italic">⇒</mo><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>×</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>μ</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>×</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>π</mi><mo>×</mo><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mstyle></mrow></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup><mo>×</mo><mn>5</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>×</mo><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>40</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q22. JEE Main 2024 (30 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

<p>Two insulated circular loop <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math> radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi></math> carrying a current of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>I</mi></math> in the anti clockwise direction as shown in figure. The magnitude of the magnetic induction at the centre will be:</p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/65c7e95d-1a1b-4058-93c2-228615f99518.png" style="width: 200px; height: 207px;"/>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><msub><mi>μ</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>I</mi></mrow><mi>a</mi></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>μ</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>a</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>μ</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mi>a</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>μ</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>I</mi></mrow><mi>a</mi></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>μ</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mi>a</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/65c7e95d-1a1b-4058-93c2-228615f99518.png" style="width: 200px; height: 207px;"/></p><p>Right hand thumb rule states that, “If you imagine holding a current carrying wire in your right-hand with your thumb pointing towards the direction of electric current flow then the direction in which your fingers curl, gives the direction of lines of force of the magnetic field”. In this case, direciton of magnetic field at the centre for both wires is as shown below.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/3983bd4a-3fc4-4873-b01d-f2d883090377.png" style="width: 250px; height: 154px;"/></p><p>Magnetic field due to a circular wire is given by, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mi>A</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>B</mi><mi>B</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>μ</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>a</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p>As both magnetic field lines at centre are perpendicular, therefore</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>∵</mo><msub><mi>B</mi><mrow><mi>n</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><msqrt><msup><mfenced><msub><mi>B</mi><mi>A</mi></msub></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mfenced><msub><mi>B</mi><mi>B</mi></msub></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><msub><mi>μ</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>a</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>μ</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mi>a</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p>

</details>

### Q23. JEE Main 2024 (27 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `3`

<p>The magnetic field at the centre of a wire loop formed by two semicircular wires of radii <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>R</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>π</mi><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> carrying current <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>I</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> as per figure given below is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>α</mi><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>. The value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>α</mi></math> is _____. (Centre <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math> is common for all segments)</p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/a46f2cde-6b61-42cd-875b-aa07113600e9.png" style="width: 250px; height: 182px;"/>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **3**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/045a49dc-e6d5-4cb3-8f86-5e20e42ef08a.png" style="width: 250px; height: 186px;"/></p><p>From the figure, it is evident that the magnetic field at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">O</mi></math> due to the straight portion of the loop is zero, only the contributions from the circular parts survive.</p><p>Also, the direction of the magnetic field due to the circular loops can be determined by the right-hand corkscrew rule.</p><p>Hence, the net magnetic field at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>O</mi></math> can be calculated as follows:</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtable columnalign="right center left" columnspacing="0px"><mtr><mtd><mi>B</mi></mtd><mtd><mo>=</mo></mtd><mtd><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>μ</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac><mfenced><mfrac><mi>π</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mo>⊗</mo><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>μ</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>R</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac><mfenced><mfrac><mi>π</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mo>⊗</mo></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd></mtd><mtd><mo>=</mo></mtd><mtd><mfenced><mrow><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>μ</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><msub><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>μ</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><msub><mi>R</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>⊗</mo></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd></mtd><mtd><mo>=</mo></mtd><mtd><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>π</mi><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup><mo>×</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>π</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>π</mi><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup><mo>×</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>×</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>⊗</mo><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd></mtd><mtd><mo>=</mo></mtd><mtd><mn>3</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup><mo>⊗</mo><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></mtd></mtr></mtable></math></p><p>Hence, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>α</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn></math>.</p>

</details>

### Q24. JEE Main 2024 (27 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `1`

<p>Two long, straight wires carry equal currents in opposite directions as shown in figure. The separation between the wires is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0</mn><mo> </mo><mi>cm</mi></math>. The magnitude of the magnetic field at a point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></math> midway between the wires is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mo> </mo><mi>μ</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>. (Given: <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>μ</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>π</mi><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo> </mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>)</p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/36a43172-c25e-4774-a993-cd3de82d3682.png" style="width: 200px; height: 176px;"/>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **160**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/36a43172-c25e-4774-a993-cd3de82d3682.png" style="width: 200px; height: 176px;"/></p><p>The direction of the magnetic field at the observation point due to the current carrying conductor is determined by the right-hand cork screw rule.</p><p>From the given diagram, it is clear that as the conductors carry current in the mutually opposite direction, the direction of the magnetic fields produced by them at point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> will be the same.</p><p>Hence, the required magnetic field at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> due to the conductors can be calculated as follows:</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtable columnalign="right center left" columnspacing="0px"><mtr><mtd><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mtd><mtd><mo>=</mo></mtd><mtd><mn>2</mn><mo>×</mo><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>μ</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></mrow></mfrac></mfenced></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd></mtd><mtd><mo>=</mo></mtd><mtd><mn>2</mn><mo>×</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>π</mi><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup><mo>×</mo><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi><mo>×</mo><mfenced><mrow><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfrac><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd></mtd><mtd><mo>=</mo></mtd><mtd><mn>160</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup><mo> </mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd></mtd><mtd><mo>=</mo></mtd><mtd><mn>160</mn><mo> </mo><mi>μT</mi></mtd></mtr></mtable></math></p>

</details>

The magnetic field produced by the current at the centre <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>O</mi></math> of the arc is ____ <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#215;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>&#8211;</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math></p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/7b9cea35-7c92-4ba4-ad93-e1a164009b0d.png" style="width: 350px; height: 136px;" /></p>

### Q26. JEE Main 2023 (08 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `2`

The magnetic intensity at the centre of a long current carrying solenoid is found to be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>3</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>. If the number of turns is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn></math> per <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>cm</mi></math>, then the current flowing through the solenoid is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>.</mo></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **2**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>The magnetic intensity <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>H</mi></math> is defined as the product of the number of turns per unit length in a coil <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>n</mi></math> and the current that it carries<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>I</mi></math>.</p><p>The magnetic field of a solenoid is given by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>n</mi><mi>i</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>H</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>n</mi><mi>i</mi></mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mfrac><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>H</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><mi>i</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>(</mo><mi>i</mi><mo>)</mo><mo>&#160;</mo></math></p><p>The given data is </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>H</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>3</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn></math> per <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>cm</mi></math></p><p>Substituting the values in equation (i)</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1000</mn><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>8</mn><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>100</mn></mfrac></mstyle></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mi>i</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>i</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q27. JEE Main 2023 (06 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

<p>A long straight wire of circular cross-section (radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi></math>) is carrying steady current <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>.</mo></math> The current <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></math> is uniformly distributed across this cross-section. The magnetic field is</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <p>inversely proportional to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>r</mi></math> in the region <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>r</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>a</mi></math> and uniform throughout in the region <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>r</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi>a</mi></math></p>
- **(B)** <p>directly proportional to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>r</mi></math> in the region <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>r</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>a</mi></math> and inversely proportional to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>r</mi></math> in the region <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>r</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi>a</mi></math></p>
- **(C)** Zero in the region <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>r</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>a</mi></math> and inversely proportional to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>r</mi></math> in the region <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>r</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi>a</mi></math>
- **(D)** <p>uniform in the region <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>r</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>a</mi></math> and inversely proportional to distance <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>r</mi></math> from the axis, in the region <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>r</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi>a</mi></math></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <p>directly proportional to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>r</mi></math> in the region <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>r</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>a</mi></math> and inversely proportional to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>r</mi></math> in the region <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>r</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi>a</mi></math></p>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>The formula to calculate the magnetic field inside the conducting wire is given by</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mtext>in&#160;</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>J</mi><mi>r</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo></math></p><p>The formula to calculate the magnetic field outside the conducting wire is given by</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mtext>out&#160;</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>J</mi><msup><mi>a</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></math></p><p>From equation (1), it can be concluded that the magnetic field is directly proportional to the radial distance inside the wire, whereas from equation (2), it can be concluded that the magnetic field is inversely proportional to the radial distance outside the wire.</p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q28. JEE Main 2023 (01 Feb Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

<p>As shown in the figure, a long straight conductor with semicircular arc of radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mn>10</mn></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>m</mi></math> is carrying current <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>I</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>3</mn><mi>A</mi></math>. The magnitude of the magnetic field. at the center <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>O</mi></math> of the arc is: (The permeability of the vacuum <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mi>NA</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math> )</p><p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/fb03d64f-fc11-4634-8037-b4082eab245e-9896307.PNG" style="width: 400px; height: 130px;" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#956;T</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#956;T</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#956;T</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#956;T</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#956;T</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The formula to calculate the magnetic field <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><msub><mi>B</mi><mi>C</mi></msub></mfenced></math> produced at the centre of a circular current carrying conductor is given by</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mi>C</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mi>o</mi></msub><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfrac><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo></math></p><p>where, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math> is the permeability of free space, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>I</mi></math> is the current through the conductor, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>r</mi></math> is the radius of the circular conductor and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#952;</mi></math> is the angle subtended by the arc at the centre.</p><p>From the given figure, it is clear that the arc of the conductor makes an angle <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#960;</mi></math> at its centre.</p><p>Substitute the known values of the parameters into equation (1) to calculate the required magnetic field.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtable columnalign="right center left" columnspacing="0px"><mtr><mtd><msub><mi>B</mi><mi>C</mi></msub></mtd><mtd><mo>=</mo></mtd><mtd><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mi>&#960;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mn>10</mn></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></mstyle></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mi>&#960;</mi></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd></mtd><mtd><mo>=</mo></mtd><mtd><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mrow><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>6</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#956;T</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></mrow></mfrac></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd></mtd><mtd><mo>=</mo></mtd><mtd><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#956;T</mi></mtd></mtr></mtable></math></p>

</details>

### Q29. JEE Main 2023 (01 Feb Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

<p>A coil is placed in magnetic field such that plane of coil is perpendicular to the direction of magnetic field. The magnetic flux through a coil can be changed:</p><p>A. By changing the magnitude of the magnetic field within the coil.</p><p>B. By changing the area of coil within the magnetic field.</p><p>C. By changing the angle between the direction of magnetic field and the plane of the coil.</p><p>D. By reversing the magnetic field direction abruptly without changing its magnitude.</p><p>Choose the most appropriate answer from the options given below:</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** A and B only
- **(B)** A, B and C only
- **(C)** A, B and D only
- **(D)** A and C only

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) A, B and C only**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Magnetic flux through a coil is given by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>=</mo><mover><mi>B</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>.</mo><mover><mi>A</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mi>B</mi><mi>A</mi><mi>cos</mi><mi>&#952;</mi></math>, where,<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math>is magnetic field, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math> is area of coil and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#952;</mi></math> is the angle between <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math>.</p><p>Thus, from above expression, we can say that magnetic flux can be changed by changing magnetic field, area of coil, change the angle between <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math>.</p><p>Hence, correct options are A, B and C.</p>

</details>

---

### Q30. JEE Main 2023 (01 Feb Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

<p>Find the magnetic field at the point P in figure. The curved portion is a semicircle connected to two long straight wires.</p><p style="text-align: justify;"><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/4c51cc7f-2edc-4039-9b98-7f1e59bd21ca-9896208.PNG" style="width: 184px; height: 220px;" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/0d630aa0-426d-4d28-97c7-d14554e5bb5d-9896208.png" style="width: 400px; height: 431px;" /></p><p>Magnitude of magnetic field due to semi-infinite portion of wire AB at point C is</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p>Magnitude of magnetic field due to semicircular arc BD at point C is</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p>Magnitude of magnetic field due to semi-infinite portion DE at point C is 0.</p><p>Using right-hand rule, it can be seen that directions of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover></math> are both outwards from the plane.</p><p>Therefore, the magnitude of net magnetic field at point C is</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math></p><p>   <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math></p>

</details>

### Q31. JEE Main 2023 (31 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

A long conducting wire having a current <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>I</mi></math> flowing through it, is bent into a circular coil of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>N</mi></math> turns. Then it is bent into a circular coil of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>n</mi></math> turns. The magnetic field is calculated at the centre of coils in both the cases. The ratio of the magnetic field in first case to that of second case is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>N</mi><mo>:</mo><mi>n</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>n</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>:</mo><msup><mi>N</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>N</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>:</mo><msup><mi>n</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>n</mi><mo>:</mo><mi>N</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>N</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>:</mo><msup><mi>n</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>As in both cases, length of the wire will remain the same, therefore</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>l</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi>N</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfenced><mi>n</mi><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mi>R</mi><mi>r</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>n</mi><mi>N</mi></mfrac></math></p><p>Now magnetic field due to single circular coil is given by, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfrac></mfenced></math>.</p><p>Therefore, required ratio of total magnetic field wil be,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>N</mi><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mi>n</mi><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfrac></mfenced></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mfrac><msup><mi>N</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>n</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac></mfenced></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q32. JEE Main 2023 (30 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

<p>As shown in the figure, a current of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> flowing in an equilateral triangle of side <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>cm</mi></math> . The magnetic field at the centroid <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>O</mi></math> of the triangle is :</p><p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/8b36ab3c-f7fc-486f-87df-3b6eeecdd2d0-9895426.PNG" style="width: 160px; height: 160px;" /></p><p>(Neglect the effect of earth&rsquo;s magnetic field)</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/8b36ab3c-f7fc-486f-87df-3b6eeecdd2d0-9895426.PNG" style="width: 160px; height: 160px;" /></p><p>Perpendicular distance of centroid from any of the sides will be, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>d</mi><mi>tan</mi><mn>60</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>d</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>cm</mi></math></p><p>Each side will subtend an angle of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>120</mn><mo>&#176;</mo></math> at the centre. Also magnetic field due to all sides will be out of plane of paper, hence total magnetic field can be written as</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;d</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><mi>sin</mi><mn>60</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mo>+</mo><mi>sin</mi><mn>60</mn><mo>&#176;</mo></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mrow><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q33. JEE Main 2023 (29 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The electric current in a circular coil of four turns produces a magnetic induction <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>32</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math> at its centre. The coil is unwound and is rewound into a circular coil of single turn, the magnetic induction at the centre of the coil by the same current will be :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>16</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Magnetic field at the centre of the coil is given by, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>.</p><p>Now in the first case:</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac></mfenced></math></p><p>and for the second case:</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>&#39;</mo><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>R</mi><mo>&#39;</mo></mrow></mfrac></math>.</p><p>As the length of the wire will remain same, therefore</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>R</mi><mo>&#39;</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfenced><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>R</mi><mo>&#39;</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>R</mi></math></p><p>Hence, required ratio will be</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><mi>B</mi><mo>&#39;</mo></mrow><mi>B</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>R</mi><mrow><mn>4</mn><msup><mi>R</mi><mo>&#39;</mo></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>16</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>B</mi><mo>&#39;</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math></p>

</details>

---

### Q34. JEE Main 2023 (29 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

<p>The magnitude of magnetic induction at mid-point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>O</mi></math> due to current arrangement as shown in figure will be</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/6bf3948f-39c3-4a8d-addd-e51319219d8d-image.png" style="width: 300px; height: 171px;" />.</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;a</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;a</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>I</mi></mrow><mi>&#960;a</mi></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>I</mi></mrow><mi>&#960;a</mi></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/6bf3948f-39c3-4a8d-addd-e51319219d8d-image.png" style="width: 300px; height: 171px;" /></p><p>The magnetic field at point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>O</mi></math> due to the two semi-infinite portions of both wires lying along line <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mi>E</mi></math> will be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn></math>.</p><p>Using right-hand rule, it can be determined that the magnetic field due to both the semi-infinite portions of wires <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mi>C</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mtext>and</mtext><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>E</mi><mi>T</mi></math> which are perpendicular to <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mi>E</mi></math> will be outwards from the plane.</p><p>The magnetic field due to one such portion will be:</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfenced><mfrac><mi>a</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mfenced></mstyle></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p>Since the magnetic field of both the portions is in same direction, the net magnetic field at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>O</mi></math> will be:</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mrow><mi>n</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfenced><mfrac><mi>a</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mfenced></mstyle></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>a</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p>

</details>

### Q35. JEE Main 2023 (29 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

<p>A single current carrying loop of wire carrying current <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>I</mi></math> flowing in anticlockwise direction seen from <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>+</mo><mi>v</mi><mi>e</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>z</mi></math> direction and lying in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mi>y</mi></math> plane is shown in figure. The plot of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi mathvariant="normal">j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math> component of magnetic field <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mi>B</mi><mi>y</mi><mo>)</mo></math> at a distance <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi></math> (less than radius of the coil) and on <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>y</mi><mi>z</mi></math> plane <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>v</mi><mi>s</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>z</mi></math> coordinate looks like</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/d526cbd6-f580-4abb-a0c2-6ecb7d3dbf1e-9894755.png" style="width: 400px; height: 259px;" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/a9cc761c-fe2d-4e45-9181-57fa277488f7-9894755_A.png" style="width: 250px; height: 143px;" />
- **(B)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/224a48c8-ee7e-43b8-a3f1-c167db3f34e3-9894755_B.png" style="width: 250px; height: 171px;" />
- **(C)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/f0cdb258-93c3-483a-8abc-f32cd35d83ab-9894755_C.png" style="width: 250px; height: 154px;" />
- **(D)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/16308e77-21ab-4b7c-8f8a-2655564c8d04-9894755_D.png" style="width: 250px; height: 136px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/f0cdb258-93c3-483a-8abc-f32cd35d83ab-9894755_C.png" style="width: 250px; height: 154px;" />**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/41bef861-efa1-45a7-86c6-cb193703a900-9894755_S.png" style="width: 300px; height: 194px;" /></p><p>Magnetic field in plane of coil is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mi>y</mi></msub><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>0</mn></math></p><p>The direction of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mover><mi>j</mi><mo>^</mo></mover></math> component of magnetic field <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mi>y</mi></msub></math> is opposite of each other in <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8211;</mo><mi>z</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>+</mo><mi>z</mi></math> positions.</p><p>So, the variation of magnetic field is shown below.</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/f0cdb258-93c3-483a-8abc-f32cd35d83ab-9894755_C.png" style="width: 250px; height: 154px;" /></p>

</details>

### Q36. JEE Main 2023 (25 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `3`

Two long parallel wires carrying currents <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>15</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> in opposite directions are placed at a distance of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>7</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>cm</mi></math> from each other. A point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> is at equidistant from both the wires such that the lines joining the point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> to the wires are perpendicular to each other. The magnitude of magnetic field at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> is ______ <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>. (Given: <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn></math>)

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **68**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/5b4264f9-eee8-4c59-ad1f-fbdfb566ca7f-9894776.PNG" style="width: 192px; height: 150px;" /></p><p>Here, the magnetic fields produced by the two wires will be perpendicular to one another.</p><p>Magnetic field due to wire carrying current <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>i</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>d</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p>and magnetic field due to wire carrying current <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>15</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>i</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>d</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p>The net magnetic field is </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mi>net</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><msqrt><msubsup><mi>B</mi><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>+</mo><msubsup><mi>B</mi><mn>2</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup></msqrt><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>d</mi></mrow></mfrac><msqrt><msubsup><mi>i</mi><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup><mo>+</mo><msubsup><mi>i</mi><mn>2</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup></msqrt></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mfenced><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac><mn>7</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac></mstyle></mfenced><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><msqrt><msup><mn>8</mn><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mn>15</mn><mn>2</mn></msup></msqrt><mfenced><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>d</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>7</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>cm</mi></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>.</p>

### Q37. JEE Main 2023 (25 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A solenoid of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1200</mn></math> turns is wound uniformly in a single layer on a glass tube <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> long and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> in diameter. The magnetic intensity at the center of the solenoid when a current of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> flows through it is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>3</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>3</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>3</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given, </p><p>Number of turns per metre <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>n</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1200</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math></p><p> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>l</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>d</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mtext>and</mtext><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>I</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math></p><p>Magnetic field at centre inside the solenoid is given by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>nI</mi></math>, where, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math> is the permeability constant.</p><p>Magnetic intensity is related to magnetic field by </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>n</mi><mi>I</mi></mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><mi>I</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mfrac><mn>1200</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>)</mo></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mn>3</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math></p>

</details>
