### Q39. JEE Main 2023 (24 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>A long solenoid is formed by winding <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>70</mn></math> turns <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mi>cm</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> . If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> current flows, then the magnetic field produced inside the solenoid is ______________.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><msub><mo>&#181;</mo><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>&#8211;</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mrow><mo>&#8211;</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><mo>)</mo></math></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1232</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>&#8211;</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>176</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>&#8211;</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>352</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>&#8211;</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>88</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>&#8211;</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>176</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>&#8211;</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Magnetic field inside solenoid <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mi>o</mi></msub><mi>n</mi><mi>i</mi></math></p><p>where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>n</mi><mo>=</mo></math> Number of turns per unit length</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>7000</mn></math> turns/m</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mtext>solenoid&#160;</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mi>o</mi></msub><mi>n</mi><mi>i</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>7000</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mn>22</mn><mn>7</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>7</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>100</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>176</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q40. JEE Main 2023 (24 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A circular loop of radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi></math> is carrying current <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>i</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>. The ratio of magnetic field at the centre of circular loop and at a distance <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi></math> from the center of the loop on its axis is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>3</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mo>:</mo><mn>2</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mo>:</mo><mn>1</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>:</mo><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mo>:</mo><mn>1</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The magnitude of magnetic field at the centre of loop is</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mtext>1&#160;</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>B</mi><mtext>centre&#160;</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mo>&#8728;</mo></msub><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8230;</mo><mfenced><mn>1</mn></mfenced></math></p><p> </p><p><img alt="" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/eb95c3c8-c674-4f50-92f7-5dea6e76919d-9884749.png" style="width: 300px; height: 209px;" /></p><p>The magnitude of magnetic field at a point at a distance <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math> from the centre along axis of loop is</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mtext>axis&#160;</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mo>&#8728;</mo></msub><mi>i</mi><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msup><mfenced><mrow><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mstyle></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mtext>2&#160;</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mo>&#8728;</mo></msub><mi>i</mi><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mfenced><msup><mn>2</mn><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></msup></mfenced><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#8230;</mo><mfenced><mn>2</mn></mfenced></math>      <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo><mtext>since</mtext><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>R</mi><mo>)</mo></math></p><p>Dividing equation 1 by equation 2, we get</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mo>:</mo><mn>1</mn></math></p>

</details>

### Q41. JEE Main 2022 (28 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The magnetic field at the center of current carrying circular loop is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math>. The magnetic field at a distance of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></math> times radius of the given circular loop from the center on its axis is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math>. The value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac></math> will be

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>9</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>4</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>12</mn><mo>:</mo><msqrt><mn>5</mn></msqrt></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>1</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo>:</mo><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>1</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Magnetic field at the centre of the loop i.e. <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p>Magnetic field on the axis of the loop is,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>i</mi><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msup><mfenced><mrow><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mstyle></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>i</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msup><mfenced><mrow><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mn>3</mn><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mstyle></msup></mrow></mfrac></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>i</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>8</mn><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>8</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p>Therefore, the ratio <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>8</mn><mn>1</mn></mfrac></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q42. JEE Main 2022 (26 Jul Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mi>X</mi></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mi>Y</mi></msub></math> are the magnetic field at the centre of two coils of two coils <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>X</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Y</mi></math> respectively, each carrying equal current. If coil <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>X</mi></math> has <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>200</mn></math> turns and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>20</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>cm</mi></math> radius and coil <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>Y</mi></math> has <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>400</mn></math> turns and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>20</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>cm</mi></math> radius, the ratio of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mi>X</mi></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mi>Y</mi></msub></math> is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>1</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>2</mn></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>1</mn></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>1</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>2</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Magnetic field at the centre of a single coil is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p>If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>N</mi></math> number of loops are kept closely <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>N</mi><mfenced><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac></mfenced></math></p><p>Therefore,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>B</mi><mi>x</mi></msub><msub><mi>B</mi><mi>y</mi></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>N</mi><mi>x</mi></msub><msub><mi>N</mi><mi>y</mi></msub></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>B</mi><mi>x</mi></msub><msub><mi>B</mi><mi>y</mi></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

---

### Q43. JEE Main 2022 (25 Jul Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The electric current in a circular coil of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn></math> turns produces a magnetic induction <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math> at its centre. The coil is unwound and is rewound into a circular coil of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn></math> turns and the same current produces a magnetic induction <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></math> at its centre.
The ratio of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mfrac></math> is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>25</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>25</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>25</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Magnetic field on the centre of a circular current coil is given by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>N</mi><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>, here, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>N</mi></math> is number of turns, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>i</mi></math> is current and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi></math> is radius of loop.</p><p>Now, for, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>N</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></math>, magnetic field, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>N</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p>For <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>N</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn></math></p><p>Radius of coil <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>N</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#215;</mo><msub><mi>R</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow><msub><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>N</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac></math></p><p>Magnetic field, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>N</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p>Thus, the ratio is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>B</mi></mrow><mn>1</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>N</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>N</mi></mrow><mn>1</mn></msub></mfrac><mfrac><msub><mi>R</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>N</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>N</mi></mrow><mn>1</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>N</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>N</mi></mrow><mn>1</mn></msub></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>B</mi></mrow><mn>1</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>25</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math></p></blockquote>

</details>

---

### Q44. JEE Main 2022 (29 Jun Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Two long current carrying conductors are placed parallel to each other at a distance of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>cm</mi></math> between them. The magnitude of magnetic field produced at mid-point between the two conductors due to current flowing in them is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>300</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>&#956;T</mi></math>. The equal current flowing in the two conductors is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>30</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> in the same direction.
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>60</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> in the opposite direction.
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>30</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> in the opposite direction.
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>300</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> in the opposite direction.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>30</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> in the opposite direction.**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/4752c0f6-4e4e-480a-b0d2-ddd73affa0a1-image.png" style="width: 130px; height: 230px;" /></p><p>Magnetic field in parallel wires is given by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>d</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p>At midway, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mtext>distance</mtext><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>d</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8757;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>I</mi></mrow><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>d</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mstyle></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>=</mo><mi>B</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>&#960;</mi></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>I</mi></mrow><mi>d</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>B</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>08</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>300</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>I</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>08</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>30</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math></p><p>As <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mrow><mi>n</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></msub><mo>&#8800;</mo><mn>0</mn></math>, so the wires are carrying current in opposite direction.  </p>

</details>

### Q45. JEE Main 2022 (28 Jun Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

<p>An infinitely long hollow conducting cylinder with radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi></math> carries a uniform current along its surface.</p><p>Choose the correct representation of magnetic field <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi>B</mi></mfenced></math>as a function of radial distance <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi>r</mi></mfenced></math> from the axis of cylinder.</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/459f2922-c39f-41c5-9045-aa6b69a8d096-image.png" style="width: 200px; height: 124px;" />
- **(B)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/b3f59dbe-c01c-4ef6-afaf-dbbd75fb44a7-image.png" style="width: 200px; height: 128px;" />
- **(C)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/32a889d5-12d2-4b67-aaa6-463a46113822-image.png" style="width: 200px; height: 137px;" />
- **(D)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/c1a991a4-0e1d-4083-a643-ad192663ef6d-image.png" style="width: 200px; height: 136px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/c1a991a4-0e1d-4083-a643-ad192663ef6d-image.png" style="width: 200px; height: 136px;" />**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>According to Ampere&#39;s law,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8750;</mo><mover><mi>B</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>&#183;</mo><mo>d</mo><mover><mi>l</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>i</mi></math></p><p>Inside the hollow cylinder, current will be zero. Therefore, magnetic field will also be zero.</p><p>Outside the cylinder,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>r</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>i</mi><mspace linebreak="newline"></mspace><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>B</mi><mo>&#8733;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi>r</mi></mfrac></math></p><p>Hence, the correct answer is D.</p>

</details>

### Q46. JEE Main 2022 (25 Jun Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A long solenoid carrying a current produces a magnetic field <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math> along its axis. If the current is doubled and the number of turns per <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>cm</mi></math> is halved, the new value of magnetic field will be equal to

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>B</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mi>B</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>B</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<blockquote><p>Magnetic field due to long solenoid along its axis is given as,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>n</mi><mi>I</mi></math>, where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>n</mi></math> is the number of turns in the solenoid per unit length, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>I</mi></math> is current in the solenoid and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math> is a constant called the permeability of free space.</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>n</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>I</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow><mrow><msub><mi>n</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><msub><mi>I</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p>According to the question, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>n</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>n</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mtext>and</mtext><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>I</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>I</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mi>B</mi><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>n</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mfenced><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac><msub><mi>n</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mstyle></mfenced></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>I</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>I</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>B</mi></math>; means, the magnetic field remains the same.</p></blockquote>

</details>

### Q47. JEE Main 2022 (25 Jun Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A long straight wire with a circular cross-section having radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi></math>, is carrying a steady current <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>I</mi></math>. The current <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>I</mi></math> is uniformly distributed across this cross-section. Then the variation of magnetic field due to current <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>I</mi></math> with distance <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>r</mi><mfenced><mrow><mi>r</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> from its centre will be

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>&#8733;</mo><mi>r</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>&#8733;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi>r</mi></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>&#8733;</mo><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>&#8733;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>&#8733;</mo><mi>r</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Using Ampere&#39;s law and noting that the steady current is uniformly distributed over the cross-section of the cylindrical wire.<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced close="]" open="["><mtable><mtr><mtd><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>I</mi><mi>r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>r</mi><mo>&#8804;</mo><mi>R</mi></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>r</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mi>R</mi></mtd></mtr></mtable></mfenced></math></p><p>Thus, magnetic field <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>&#8733;</mo><mi>r</mi></math> from the centre for <math><mi>r</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi>R</mi></math>.</p>

</details>

### Q48. JEE Main 2022 (24 Jun Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

The magnetic field at the centre of a circular coil of radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>r</mi></math>, due to current I flowing through it, is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math>. The magnetic field at a point along the axis at a distance <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math> from the centre is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>B</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mi>B</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced><mfrac><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>5</mn></msqrt></mfrac></mfenced><mn>3</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced><mfrac><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></mfrac></mfenced><mn>3</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msup><mfenced><mfrac><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>5</mn></msqrt></mfrac></mfenced><mn>3</mn></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Magnetic field at the centre of the circle is, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p>Magnetic field at the point on the axis is, </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>i</mi><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msup><mfenced><mrow><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mstyle></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>i</mi><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msup><mfenced><mrow><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mfenced><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mstyle></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mstyle></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#8658;</mo><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msup><mfenced><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>4</mn></mfrac></mstyle></mfenced><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mstyle></msup><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><msup><mfenced><mfrac><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>5</mn></msqrt></mfrac></mfenced><mn>3</mn></msup><mi>B</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q49. JEE Main 2021 (01 Sep Shift 2)

*Type:* `numerical` | *Level:* `3`

<p>The temperature of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>00</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mol</mi></math> of an ideal diatomic gas is increased by <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>40</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math> without changing the pressure of the gas. The molecules in the gas rotate but do not oscillate. If the ratio of change in internal energy of the gas to the amount of workdone by the gas is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mi>x</mi><mn>10</mn></mfrac><mo>.</mo></math> Then the value of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi></math> (round off to the nearest integer) is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mi>_</mi><mo>.</mo></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>(</mo></math> Given<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced open=""><mrow><mi>R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>8</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>31</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">J</mi><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>mol</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></math></p>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Value:** **25**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>First, recall the formula of change in internal energy in terms of molar specific heat at constant volume and change in temperature, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>V</mi></msub><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>T</mi></math>, for diatomic gas, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>V</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>f</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>R</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>R</mi></math>, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mo>&#8710;</mo><mi>U</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>n</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mi>R</mi><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>T</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>U</mi></mrow><mi>W</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>T</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>n</mi><mi>R</mi><mi>&#916;</mi><mi>T</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>5</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>25</mn><mn>10</mn></mfrac></math></p><p>So, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>25</mn></math></p>

</details>

### Q50. JEE Main 2021 (27 Aug Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A coaxial cable consists of an inner wire of radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi></math> surrounded by an outer shell of inner and outer radii <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>b</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>c</mi></math> respectively. The inner wire carries an electric current <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>i</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math> which is distributed uniformly across cross-sectional area. The outer shell carries an equal current in opposite direction and distributed uniformly. What will be the ratio of the magnetic field at a distance <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></math> from the axis when <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi>i</mi></mfenced></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mi>i</mi><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">b</mi></math> ?

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msup><mi>a</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msup><mi>b</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mi>a</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mrow><msup><mi>b</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mi>a</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>a</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>a</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>I</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>(</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><msup><mo>)</mo><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>I</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>a</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac></math></p>

</details>

### Q51. JEE Main 2021 (26 Aug Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

The fractional change in the magnetic field intensity at a distance <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>r</mi></math> from centre on the axis of current carrying coil of radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi></math> to the magnetic field intensity at the centre of the same coil is: (Take <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mo>&#60;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></math>)

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mfrac><msup><mi>a</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mfrac><msup><mi>a</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mfrac><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>a</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mfrac><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>a</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mfrac><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>a</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>As we know,</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mi>axis</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msup><mi>iR</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msup><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mtext>where</mtext><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>R</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>&#160;</mo></math> </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mtext>centre&#160;</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>a</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mtext>axis&#160;</mtext></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msup><mi>ia</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msup><mfenced ### Q53. JEE Main 2021 (18 Mar Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A loop of flexible wire of irregular shape carrying current is placed in an external magnetic field. Identify the effect of the field on the wire.

**Options:**

- **(A)** Loop assumes circular shape with its plane normal to the field.
- **(B)** Loop assumes circular shape with its plane parallel to the field.
- **(C)** Wire gets stretched to become straight.
- **(D)** Shape of the loop remains unchanged.

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) Loop assumes circular shape with its plane normal to the field.**

**Detailed Solution:**

Every part (<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>d</mi><mi>l</mi></math> ) of the wire is pulled by the force <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>i</mi><mo>(</mo><mi>d</mi><mi>l</mi><mo>)</mo><mi>B</mi></math> acting perpendicular to the current <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#38;</mo></math> magnetic field giving it the shape of a circle.

</details>

### Q54. JEE Main 2021 (18 Mar Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

<p>Four identical long solenoids <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>,</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">D</mi></math> are connected to each other as shown in the figure. If the magnetic field at the center of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math> the field at the center of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math> would be : (Assume that the magnetic field is confined with in the volume of respective solenoid).</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/5fa139c1-b659-4527-8485-0b3b20338088-image.png" style="width: 373px; height: 165px;" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>12</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>9</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/36208a61-da00-4341-894d-55ed7958c9c8-image.png" style="width: 373px; height: 168px;" /></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#981;</mi><mo>&#8733;</mo><mi>i</mi></math><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mi>B</mi><mo>&#8733;</mo><mi>i</mi></math>, so, the field at the center of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>C</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>.</p>

</details>

### Q55. JEE Main 2021 (17 Mar Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

<p>A hairpin like shape as shown in figure is made by bending a long current carrying wire. What is the magnitude of a magnetic field at point <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi></math> which lies on the centre of the semicircle ?</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/7915e410-fb05-4413-a249-76f3a0b7ec86-image.png" style="width: 262px; height: 151px;" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>&#960;</mi><mo>)</mo></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>&#960;</mi><mo>)</mo></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>&#960;</mi><mo>)</mo></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>&#960;</mi><mo>)</mo></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>&#960;</mi><mo>)</mo></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msub><mi>B</mi><mrow><mi>st</mi><mo>.</mo><mi>wire</mi></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>B</mi><mi>loop</mi></msub></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfenced><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow></mfrac></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>r</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>&#960;</mi><mo>)</mo></math></p>

</details>

### Q56. JEE Main 2021 (17 Mar Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>A solenoid of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1000</mn></math> turns per metre has a core with relative permeability <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>500</mn><mo>.</mo></math> Insulated windings of the solenoid carry an electric current of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>.</mo></math> The magnetic flux density produced by the solenoid is:</p><p>(Permeability of free space<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">H</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math>)</p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi>&#960;</mi></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></msup><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>5</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mrow><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced><mi>&#960;</mi></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>&#956;</mi><mi>n</mi><mi>I</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mi>r</mi></msub><mi>n</mi><mi>I</mi></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>500</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>1000</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>5</mn></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>&#960;</mi></math> Tesla</p>

</details>

### Q57. JEE Main 2021 (25 Feb Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

Magnetic fields at two points on the axis of a circular coil at a distance of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>05</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> from the centre are in the ratio <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>8</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>1</mn></math>. The radius of coil is _______ .

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>15</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>1</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>We know, the magnetic field on the axis of a current carrying circular ring is given by</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>N</mi><mi>I</mi><mi>A</mi></mrow><msup><mfenced><mrow><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8756;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mrow><mo>&#160;</mo><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>8</mn><mn>1</mn></mfrac><mo>=</mo><msup><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mfrac><mrow><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><mrow><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>05</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfrac></mfenced><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mfenced open="[" close="]"><mrow><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>05</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfenced open="[" mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>=</mo><msup><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>-</mo><msup><mfenced><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>.</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mn>2</mn></msup></math></p><p><math ### Q58. JEE Main 2020 (04 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

<p>A wire <math><mi>A</mi></math>, bent in the shape of an arc of a circle, carrying a current of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> and having radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>cm</mi></math> and another wire <math><mi>B</mi></math>, also bent in the shape of an arc of a circle, carrying a current of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> and having radius of <math><mrow><mn>4</mn><mtext>&#160;cm</mtext></mrow></math>, are placed as shown in the figure. The ratio of the magnetic fields due to the wires <math><mtext>A</mtext></math> and <math><mtext>B</mtext></math> at the common centre <math><mtext>O</mtext></math> is:</p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/89ed84c3-9a52-4826-aea4-76a9dd140da8-image.png" style="width: 200px; height: 175px;" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>4</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>6</mn></math>
- **(B)** <math><mrow><mn>6</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></math>
- **(C)** <math><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>5</mn></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>6</mn><mo>:</mo><mn>5</mn></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>Given,</p><p>Current in the arc <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>i</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math></p><p>Current in the arc <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>i</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math></p><p>Radius of arc <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>R</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>cm</mi></math></p><p>Radius of arc <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math> is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>R</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>cm</mi></math></p><p>Magnetic field at the centre of circular current carrying arc is given by</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn mathvariant="italic">0</mn></msub><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn mathvariant="italic">4</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfenced><mi>&#952;</mi></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>)</mo></math> </p><p>Where, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#952;</mi></math> is the angle subtended at centre.</p><p>Magnetic field at the centre due to arc <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math> is given by </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mi>A</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><msub><mi>&#960;R</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mfenced><mn>2</mn></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mi>B</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><msub><mi>&#960;R</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>3</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mo>.</mo><mfenced><mn>3</mn></mfenced></math></p><p>Dividing equation (2) by (1), we get</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>B</mi><mi>A</mi></msub><msub><mi>B</mi><mi>B</mi></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>i</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><msub><mi>i</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>R</mi><mn mathvariant="italic">2</mn></msub><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mo>&#8722;</mo><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mstyle></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><msub><mi>R</mi><mn mathvariant="italic">1</mn></msub><mfenced><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mo>&#8722;</mo><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mn>3</mn></mfrac></mstyle></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>B</mi><mi>A</mi></msub><msub><mi>B</mi><mi>B</mi></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>2</mn><mn>3</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mn>4</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi></mrow><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mn>3</mn><mrow><mn>5</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi></mrow></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><msub><mi>B</mi><mi>A</mi></msub><msub><mi>B</mi><mi>B</mi></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>6</mn><mn>5</mn></mfrac></math></p>

</details>

### Q59. JEE Main 2020 (03 Sep Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

Magnitude of magnetic field (in SI units) at the centre of a hexagonal shape coil of side <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>10</mn><mo>&#160;</mo><mi>cm</mi><mo>,</mo><mn>50</mn></math> turns and carrying current<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>I</mi></math>  (Ampere) in units of <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>I</mi></mrow><mi>&#960;</mi></mfrac></math> is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>250</mn><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>50</mn><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></math>
- **(C)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>500</mn><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></math>
- **(D)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>5</mn><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>500</mn><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>The magnetic field due to the current-carrying finite length wire is given by</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>sin</mi><msub><mi>&#952;</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>+</mo><mi>sin</mi><msub><mi>&#952;</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p>Here for hexagonal shape coil, <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>&#952;</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mi>&#952;</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>30</mn><mo>&#176;</mo></math>.</p><p>So, the net magnetic field at the centre will be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>50</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>6</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mfenced><mrow><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac><mn>10</mn><mn>100</mn></mfrac></mstyle><mi>cos</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>30</mn><mo>&#176;</mo></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfrac><mfenced close="]" open="["><mrow><mi>sin</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>30</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mo>+</mo><mi>sin</mi><mo>&#160;</mo><mn>30</mn><mo>&#176;</mo></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>75</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>10</mn><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfenced><mrow><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1500</mn><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></mfrac><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>i</mi></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi></mfrac><mspace linebreak="newline"/><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>&#160;</mo><mo>=</mo><mfenced><mrow><mn>500</mn><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfenced><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>i</mi></mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi></mfrac></math></p>

</details>

### Q60. JEE Main 2020 (08 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A very long wire ABDMNDC is shown in figure carrying current I. AB and BC parts are straight, long and at right angle. At D wire forms a circular turn DMND of radius R. AB, BC parts are tangential to circular turn at N and D. Magnetic filed at the center of circle is:
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/1b8fd30d-aa69-49f9-be76-c4db640829ef-image.png" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>μ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>π</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>μ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>π</mi><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>μ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>π</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>μ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>μ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>π</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/d2a273e3-518c-42f8-9c13-1442028c3410-image.png" style="width: 300px; height: 317px;" />
<math><mover accent="true"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>B</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mover accent="true"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>B</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mover accent="true"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>B</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mover accent="true"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>B</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mover accent="true"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>B</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msub></math>
<math><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>&#956;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">sin</mi></mrow><mo>&#8289;</mo><mrow><mn>90</mn></mrow></mrow><mo>&#176;</mo><mo>-</mo><mi>s</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>n</mi><mn>45</mn><mo>&#176;</mo></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mo stretchy="false">&#10754;</mo><mrow></mrow></mrow><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>&#956;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac><mrow><mo stretchy="false">&#10752;</mo><mrow></mrow></mrow><mo>+</mo></math> <math><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>&#956;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>(</mo><mi>s</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>n</mi><mn>45</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mo>+</mo><mi>s</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>n</mi><mn>90</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mo>)</mo><mo>&#10752;</mo></math>
<math><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>&#956;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>&#956;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>&#956;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mo stretchy="false">&#10752;</mo><mrow></mrow></mrow></math>
<math><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>&#956;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mo stretchy="false">&#10752;</mo><mrow></mrow></mrow><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>&#956;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mo>+</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mo stretchy="false">&#10752;</mo><mrow></mrow></mrow><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>&#956;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow></mfenced><mrow><mo stretchy="false">&#10752;</mo><mrow></mrow></mrow></math>

</details>

### Q61. JEE Main 2020 (07 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A long solenoid of radius <math><mi>R</mi></math> carries a time <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> dependent current <math><mi>I</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mi>t</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> . A ring of radius <math><mn>2</mn><mi>R</mi></math> is placed coaxially near its middle. During the time interval <math><mn>0</mn><mo>≤</mo><mi>t</mi><mo>≤</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mi></mi></math> the induced current <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></math> and the induced <math><mi>E</mi><mi>M</mi><mi>F</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></math> in the ring change as:

**Options:**

- **(A)** Direction of <math><msub><mrow><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow></msub></math> remains unchanged and <math><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow></msub></math> is maximum at <math><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0.5</mn></math>
- **(B)** At <math><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0.25</mn></math> direction of <math><msub><mrow><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow></msub></math> reverses and <math><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow></msub></math> is maximum
- **(C)** Direction of <math><msub><mrow><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow></msub></math> remains unchanged and <math><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow></msub></math> is zero at <math><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0.25</mn></math>
- **(D)** At <math><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0.5</mn></math> direction of <math><msub><mrow><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow></msub></math> reverses and <math><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow></msub></math> is zero

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) At <math><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0.5</mn></math> direction of <math><msub><mrow><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow></msub></math> reverses and <math><msub><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow></msub></math> is zero**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mo>-</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>
<math><mi mathvariant="normal">&#981;</mi><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>
<math><mi mathvariant="normal">&#981;</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math>
<math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#981;</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal"></mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></mfenced></math>
<math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math> at <math><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
And <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">V</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mi>e&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi>f&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

</details>

### Q62. JEE Main 2019 (12 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

<p>Find the magnetic field at point <math><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></math> due to a straight line segment <math><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></math> of length <math><mn>6</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> carrying a current of <math><mn>5</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi><mo>.</mo></math> (See figure) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mn>10</mn><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#160;</mo><msup><mi>NA</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></math></p><p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/d0887e68-cfd5-4ecb-acaf-eb4449ab676e-image.png" style="width: 268px; height: 253px;" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>1.5</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></msup><mi> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math><mn>2.0</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></msup><mi> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>3.0</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></msup><mi> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>2.5</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></msup><mi> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math><mn>1.5</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></msup><mi> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><img alt="12.04.2019 Shift 2 Synergy JEE Mains D43" src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/12.04.2019%20Shift%202_Synergy_JEE%20Mains_D43.PNG" /></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>&#952;</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mn>37</mn><mn>0</mn></msup></math>
<math><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>
<math><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mo>[</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mn>37</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mn>37</mn><mo>&#176;</mo><mo>]</mo></math>
<math><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfrac><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></math>
<math><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>1.5</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math></p>

</details>
