### Q63. JEE Main 2019 (12 Apr Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A thin ring of <math><mn>10</mn><mi>&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> radius carries a uniformly distributed charge. The ring rotates at a constant angular speed of <math><mn>40</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#160;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msup></math> about its axis, perpendicular to its plane. Is the magnetic field its centre is <math><mn>3.8</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>9</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math> , then the charge carried by the ring is close to <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal">N</mi><mo>/</mo><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced><mo>.</mo></math>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>4</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math><mn>3</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>2</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>7</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math><mn>3</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">C</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">q</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">q</mi></mrow><mrow><mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#969;</mi></mrow></mrow></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">q</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#969;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">q</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#969;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
<math><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">q</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>40</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mo>(</mo><mn>0.1</mn><mo>)</mo></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mn>3.8</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>9</mn></mrow></msup></math>
<math><mi mathvariant="normal">q</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>400</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mn>3.8</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>
<math><mi mathvariant="normal">q</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow></msup></math> C

</details>

### Q64. JEE Main 2019 (10 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

The magnitude of the magnetic field at the centre of an equilateral triangular loop of side <math><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math> which is carrying a current of <math><mn>10</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></math> is:

[Take <math><mi> </mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">μ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">N </mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math> ]

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>3</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">μ</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math><mn>1</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">μ</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><mn>18</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">μ</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><mn>9</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">μ</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) <math><mn>18</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">μ</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/d61b559a-60b7-43f9-992a-b79c18490ebb-image17.png" style="height: 179px; width: 180px;" />

The perpendicular distance of centroid from any side, <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">m</mi></math>
Magnetic field due to one side.
<math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">µ</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>[</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">θ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">θ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>]</mo></math>
<math><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">μ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mo>(</mo><mn>10</mn><mo>)</mo></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfrac><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><msup><mrow><mn>30</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>°</mo></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><msup><mrow><mn>30</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>°</mo></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></math>
<math><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>5</mn><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi></mrow></mfrac><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">μ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><msqrt><mn>3</mn></msqrt></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>15</mn><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">μ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
Net magnetic field <math><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>45</mn></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi></mrow></mfrac><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">μ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>45</mn></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>×</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>π</mi><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup></math>
<math><mo>=</mo><mn>180</mn><mo>×</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>
<math><mo>=</mo><mn>18</mn><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mi mathvariant="normal">μ</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">T</mi></math>

</details>

### Q65. JEE Main 2019 (09 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

A very long solenoid of radius <math><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></math> is carrying current <math><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#945;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></msup><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">k</mi><mo>&#62;</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>,</mo></math> as a function of time <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mo>&#8805;</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>.</mo></math> Counterclockwise current is taken to be positive. A circular conducting coil of radius <math><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></math> is placed in the equitorial plane of the solenoid and concentric with the solenoid. The current induced in the outer coil is correctly depicted, as a function of time, by:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/2772625d-6549-4679-a453-67c38f99961e-image.png" style="width: 500px; height: 244px;" />
- **(B)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/97d17580-0ca6-4046-9a68-f1827558f2c0-image.png" style="width: 500px; height: 235px;" />
- **(C)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/9ac4858e-a388-4bff-b296-f74f23147782-image.png" style="width: 400px; height: 212px;" />
- **(D)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/a95b4890-2190-47e5-88b4-375746a05950-image.png" style="width: 400px; height: 203px;" />

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/97d17580-0ca6-4046-9a68-f1827558f2c0-image.png" style="width: 500px; height: 235px;" />**

**Detailed Solution:**

<math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#981;</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">o</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">r</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#945;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#949;</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#981;</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">K</mi><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>(</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#945;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></msup><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#945;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></mfenced><mo>)</mo></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">&#945;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></msup><mfenced open="[" close="]" separators="|"><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>-</mo><mi>&#945;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">s</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
At <math><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo></math> <math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">i</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">u</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">c</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">v</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></math>
From the above equation and initial value of current we can say the correct graph is B.

</details>

### Q66. JEE Main 2019 (08 Apr Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Two very long, straight, and insulated wires are kept at <math><msup><mrow><mn>90</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>o</mi></mrow></msup></math> angle from each other in <math><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi><mo>-</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">p</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">l</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi></math> as shown in figure.
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/76ae72b4-072c-4a36-85a1-001db02031b0-image2.png" />
These wires carry currents of equal magnitude <math><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></math> , whose direction are shown in the figure. The net magnetic field at point <math><mi mathvariant="normal">P</mi></math> will be:

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">μ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mo>+</mo><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">μ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">μ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow></mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal"> </mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">x</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>+</mo><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">y</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover></mrow></mfenced></math>
- **(D)** Zero

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) Zero**

**Detailed Solution:**

Magnetic field due to wire along x-axis:
<math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow></mfrac><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover></math>
Magnetic field due to wire along z-axis:
<math><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">&#956;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">I</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>(</mo><mo>-</mo><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">z</mi></mrow><mo>^</mo></mover><mo>)</mo></math>
<math><msub><mrow><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mrow><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover></mrow><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">n</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">e</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">t</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mrow><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>+</mo><msub><mrow><mover accent="true"><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></mrow><mo>&#8594;</mo></mover></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>

</details>

### Q67. JEE Main 2019 (12 Jan Shift 1)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

As shown in the figure, two infinitely long, identical wires are bent by <math><msup><mrow><mn>90</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>o</mi></mrow></msup></math> and placed in such a way that the segments <math><mi>L</mi><mi>P</mi></math> and <math><mi>Q</mi><mi>M</mi></math> are along the <math><mi>x</mi></math> - axis, while segments <math><mi>P</mi><mi>S</mi></math> and <math><mi>Q</mi><mi>N</mi></math> are parallel to the <math><mi>y</mi></math> - axis. If <math><mi>O</mi><mi>P</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>O</mi><mi>Q</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>c</mi><mi>m</mi><mo>,</mo></math> and the magnitude of the magnetic field at <math><mi>O</mi></math> is <math><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>T</mi><mo>,</mo></math> and the two wires carry equal currents (see figure), the magnitude of the current in each wire and the direction of the magnetic field at <math><mi>O</mi></math> will be <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>&#956;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup><mi>N</mi><msup><mrow><mi>A</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced><mo>:</mo></math>

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/gqbtbpdqtvjbtvpiroavdkzsrhwiilclqtrldrqttcuvhgrquw.png" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mn>20</mn><mi> </mi><mi>A</mi><mo>,</mo></math>  perpendicular into the page
- **(B)** <math><mn>20</mn><mi> </mi><mi>A</mi><mo>,</mo></math>  perpendicular out of the page
- **(C)** <math><mn>40</mn><mi> </mi><mi>A</mi><mo>,</mo></math>  perpendicualr into the page
- **(D)** <math><mn>40</mn><mi> </mi><mi>A</mi><mo>,</mo></math>  perpendicular out of the page

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math><mn>20</mn><mi> </mi><mi>A</mi><mo>,</mo></math>  perpendicular into the page**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p>As we know that the magnetic field due to a infinite wire at a distance of d is given as </p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>d</mi></mrow></mfrac></mfenced></math></p><p>but there are two wires so that the net magnetic field is given as</p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mfenced separators="|"><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>d</mi></mrow></mfrac></mfenced></math>

<math><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><msup><mrow><mn>10</mn></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mn>7</mn></mrow></msup><mo>&#215;</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mo>&#215;</mo><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>100</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math><mi>i</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>20</mn><mi>A</mi></math></p>

</details>

### Q68. JEE Main 2019 (09 Jan Shift 2)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

One of the two identical conducting wires of length <math><mi>L</mi></math> is bent in the form of a circular loop and the other one into a circular coil of <math><mi>N</mi></math> identical turns. If the same current is passed in both, the ratio of the magnetic field at the centre of the loop <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>B</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>L</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced></math> to that at the centre of the coil <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>B</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>C</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mfenced><mo>,</mo></math> i.e. <math><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>B</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>L</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>B</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>C</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac></math> will be

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>N</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>N</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math><mi>N</mi></math>
- **(D)** <math><msup><mrow><mi>N</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>N</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

For a single loop, radius

<math><msub><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>L</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mi>L</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>r</mi><mn>1</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>L</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi></math>  ...(1)

For a circular coil, radius

<math><msub><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>L</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>N</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mi>c</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>I</mi><mi>N</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>I</mi><mi>N</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>L</mi></mrow></mfrac><mn>2</mn><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>N</mi></math>  ...(2)

<math><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>B</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>L</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>B</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>C</mi></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>N</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac></math>             (from eqns (1) and (2))

</details>

### Q69. JEE Main 2018 (15 Apr Shift 2 Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A current of $1 \mathrm{~A}$ is flowing on the sides of an equilateral triangle of side $4.5 \times 10^{-2} \mathrm{~m}$. The magnetic field at the centre of the triangle will be:

**Options:**

- **(A)** $4 \times 10^{-5} \mathrm{~Wb} / \mathrm{m}^2$
- **(B)** Zero
- **(C)** $2 \times 10^{-5} \mathrm{~Wb} / \mathrm{m}^2$
- **(D)** $8 \times 10^{-5} \mathrm{~Wb} / \mathrm{m}^2$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $4 \times 10^{-5} \mathrm{~Wb} / \mathrm{m}^2$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Here, side of the triangle, $l=4.5 \times 10^{-2} \mathrm{~m}$, current, $\mathrm{I}=1 \mathrm{~A}$
magnetic field at the centre of the triangle ' $O$ ' $B=$ ?
From figure, $\tan 60^{\circ}=\sqrt{3}=\frac{1}{2 d}$
$$
\Rightarrow d=\frac{l}{2 \sqrt{3}}=\left(\frac{4.5 \times 10^{-2}}{2 \sqrt{3}}\right) \mathrm{m}
$$
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/Oav-57KquXqyAVJHCtfO3SzjcrnSNWiiT9TN4zjBeoU.original.fullsize.png">

Magnetic field, $B=\frac{\mu_0 i}{4 \pi d}\left(\cos \theta_1+\cos \theta_2\right)$
Putting value of $\mu=4 \pi \times 10^{-7}$ and $\theta_1$ and $\theta_2$ we will get net magenetic field $=3 \times B=4 \times 10^{-5} \mathrm{~Wb} / \mathrm{m}^2$

</details>

A Helmholtz coil has a pair of loops, each with <math> <mi>N</mi> </math> turns and radius <math> <mi>R</mi> </math>. They are placed coaxially at distance <math> <mi>R</mi> </math> and the same current <math> <mi>I</mi> </math> flows through the loops in the same direction. The magnitude of the magnetic field at <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>P</mi><mo>,</mo></math> midway between the centres <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn mathvariant="italic">A</mn></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>C</mi><mo>,</mo></math> is given by [Refer to figure given below]:
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/42a31e35-345f-496d-aec5-a8c16b0fd765-image.png" style="width: 350px; height: 173px;" />

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mi> </mi><mfrac><mrow><mn>8</mn><mi>N</mi><msub><mrow><mi>μ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow><mrow><mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mrow></mrow></msup><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>N</mi><msub><mrow><mi>μ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow><mrow><mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mrow></mrow></msup><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math><mi> </mi><mfrac><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>N</mi><msub><mrow><mi>μ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow><mrow><mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mrow></mrow></msup><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math><mi> </mi><mfrac><mrow><mn>8</mn><mi>N</mi><msub><mrow><mi>μ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow><mrow><mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mrow></mrow></msup><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(D) <math><mi> </mi><mfrac><mrow><mn>8</mn><mi>N</mi><msub><mrow><mi>μ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow><mrow><mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mrow></mrow></msup><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Magnetic field at point P, due to both coils, <math><msub><mrow><mi>B</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><mfrac><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>&#956;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mi>N</mi><mi>I</mi><msup><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msup><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo>+</mo><mfrac><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mrow></mrow></msup></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced> <mrow> <mo>=</mo><mfrac> <mrow> <msub> <mi>&#956;</mi> <mn>0</mn> </msub> <mi>N</mi><mi>I</mi><msup> <mi>R</mi> <mn>2</mn> </msup> </mrow> <mrow> <mfrac> <mrow> <msup> <mn>5</mn> <mrow> <mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow> </mrow> </msup> </mrow> <mn>8</mn> </mfrac> <msup> <mi>R</mi> <mn>3</mn> </msup> </mrow> </mfrac> <mo>=</mo></mrow><mi>&#160;</mi><mfrac><mrow><mn>8</mn><mi>N</mi><msub><mrow><mi>&#956;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mi>I</mi></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mn>5</mn></mrow><mrow><mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mrow></mrow></msup><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>.

</details>

### Q71. JEE Main 2018 (15 Apr Shift 1 Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A Helmholtz coil has pair of loops, each with $N$ turns and radius $R$. They are placed coaxially at distance $R$ and the same current $I$ flows through the loops in the same direction. The magnitude of magnetic field at $P$, midway between the centres $A$ and $C$, is given by (Refer to figure):
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/eYaTevxoqNYrsgBy7lykYX9p4BekefDjSVC7utNAhP0.original.fullsize.png">

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{4 N \mu_0 I}{5^{3 / 2} R}$
- **(B)** $\frac{8 N \mu_0 I}{5^{3 / 2} R}$
- **(C)** $\frac{4 N \mu_0 I}{5^{1 / 2} R}$
- **(D)** $\frac{8 N \mu_0 I}{5^{1 / 2} R}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\frac{8 N \mu_0 I}{5^{3 / 2} R}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Point $P$ is situated at the mid-point of the line joining the centres of the circular wires which have same radii (R). The magnetic fields $(\vec{B})$ at $\mathrm{P}$ due to the currents in the wires are in same direction.
Magnitude of magnetic field at point, $P$
$B=2\left\{\frac{\mu_0 N I R^2}{2\left(R^2+\frac{R^2}{4}\right)^{3 / 2}}\right\}$
$=\frac{\mu_0 N I R^2}{\frac{5^{3 / 2}}{8}}=\frac{8 \mu_0 N I}{5^{3 / 2} R}$

</details>

### Q72. JEE Main 2016 (10 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

A conducting metal circular-wire-loop of radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>r</mi></math> is placed perpendicular to a magnetic field which varies with time as <math><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mi>B</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><msup><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>&#964;</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></msup><mo>,</mo></math> where <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>and</mi><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>&#964;</mi></math> are constants at time <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></math>. If the resistance of the loop is <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi></math>, then the heat generated in the loop after a long time <math><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>&#8594;</mo><mi>&#8734;</mi></mrow></mfenced></math> is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mfrac><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><msubsup><mrow><mi>B</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msubsup></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#964;</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msup><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>4</mn></msup><msubsup><mi>B</mi><mn>0</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#964;</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfrac><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>π</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><msubsup><mrow><mi>B</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msubsup><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>τ</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfrac><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>π</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><msubsup><mrow><mi>B</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msubsup></mrow><mrow><mi>τ</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><mrow><msup><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>4</mn></msup><msubsup><mi>B</mi><mn>0</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#964;</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

Here, <math><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><msub><mrow><mi>B</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><msup><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>&#964;</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></msup></math>
Area of the circular loop, <math><mi>A</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>&#960;</mi><msup><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></math>
Flux linked with the loop at any time <math><mi>&#160;</mi><mi>t</mi></math> ,
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">&#981;</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>B</mi><mi>A</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>&#960;</mi><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msub><mi>B</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><msup><mi>e</mi><mrow><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mi>t</mi><mrow><mi>&#964;</mi><mi>&#160;</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></msup></math>
Emf induced in the loop, <math><mi>&#949;</mi><mo>=</mo><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">&#981;</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>d</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mi>&#960;</mi><msup><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><msub><mrow><mi>B</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>&#964;</mi></mrow></mfrac><msup><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>&#964;</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></msup></math>
Net heat generated in the loop
<math><mrow><munderover><mo stretchy="false">&#8747;</mo><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>&#8734;</mi></mrow></munderover><mrow><mfrac><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>&#949;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac><mi>d</mi><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><msubsup><mrow><mi>B</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msubsup></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>&#964;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac><mrow><munderover><mo stretchy="false">&#8747;</mo><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>&#8734;</mi></mrow></munderover><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>&#964;</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></msup><mi>d</mi><mi>t</mi></mrow></mrow></mrow></mrow></math>
<math><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>&#960;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><msup><mrow><mi>r</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn></mrow></msup><msubsup><mrow><mi>B</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msubsup></mrow><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>&#964;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><mrow><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>&#964;</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></mfenced></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><msubsup><mrow><mfenced close="]" open="[" separators="|"><mrow><msup><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow><mrow><mo>-</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>t</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>&#964;</mi></mrow></mfrac></mrow></msup></mrow></mfenced></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>&#8734;</mi></mrow></msubsup></math>
<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mo>-</mo><msup><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>4</mn></msup><msubsup><mi>B</mi><mn>0</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msup><mi>&#964;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mi>&#964;</mi><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>-</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msup><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><msup><mi>r</mi><mn>4</mn></msup><msubsup><mi>B</mi><mn>0</mn><mn>2</mn></msubsup></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>&#964;</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

</details>

### Q73. JEE Main 2016 (03 Apr)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Two identical wires <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math>, each of length <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>l</mi></math>, carry the same current <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>I</mi></math> . Wire <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>A</mi></math> is bent into a circle of radius <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi></math> and wire <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>B</mi></math> is bent to form a square of side <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>a</mi></math>. If <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></msub></math> and <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></msub></math> are the values of magnetic field at the centres of the circle and square respectively, then the ratio <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>B</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">A</mi></msub><msub><mi>B</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">B</mi></msub></mfrac></math> is

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msup><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mn>16</mn></mfrac></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfrac><mrow><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>8</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfrac><mrow><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>8</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfrac><mrow><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>16</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></math>

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) <math><mfrac><mrow><msup><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">π</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup></mrow><mrow><mn>8</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></math>**

**Detailed Solution:**

<p><img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/7f991d0b-0257-4e5d-8cdc-d97e0d6dfbbf-image.png" style="width: 200px; height: 65px;" /></p><p>As length of both the wires is same, <math><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>4</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi></mrow><mn>4</mn></mfrac></math></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>&#8658;</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">a</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">R</mi></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi mathvariant="normal">&#960;</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac></math>
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/5966f627-2dd1-410f-95c5-8b519b475b42-d48.png" style="width: 200px; height: 88px;" /></p><p><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mi>A</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math> and 

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>B</mi><mi>B</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>a</mi></mrow></mfrac><mfenced separators="|"><mrow><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfenced></math>

Therefore, ratio will be <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mfrac><msub><mi>B</mi><mi>A</mi></msub><msub><mi>B</mi><mi>B</mi></msub></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>i</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>&#215;</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>a</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mi>&#160;</mi><msub><mi>&#956;</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mi>i</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

<math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mrow><mi>&#960;</mi><mi>a</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>4</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mrow><mn>4</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac><mfenced separators="|"><mfrac><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>2</mn></mfrac></mfenced><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msup><mi>&#960;</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mrow><mn>8</mn><msqrt><mn>2</mn></msqrt></mrow></mfrac></math></p>

</details>

If the wire subtends an angle <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mn>2</mn><msub><mi>&#952;</mi><mi>o</mi></msub></math> at the center of the circle (of which it forms an arch) then the tension in the wire is:
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/c2919695-bddb-4d0a-8b5a-6b7c342cb3d6-image.png" style="width: 200px; height: 175px;" /></p>

**Options:**

- **(A)** <math><mi>I</mi><mi>B</mi><mi>R</mi></math>
- **(B)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>I</mi><mi>B</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>s</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>n</mi><msub><mrow><mi>θ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(C)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>I</mi><mi>B</mi><mi>R</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>s</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>n</mi><msub><mrow><mi>θ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac></math>
- **(D)** <math><mfrac><mrow><mi>I</mi><mi>B</mi><mi>R</mi><msub><mrow><mi>θ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mrow><mi>s</mi><mi>i</mi><mi>n</mi><msub><mrow><mi>θ</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mfrac></math>

<details>

<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/64be2bb3-1c3e-40c4-90b0-cbe674f7f028-image.png" style="width: 200px; height: 191px;" />

Then magnetic force <math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi mathvariant="normal">d</mi><mi>F</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>I</mi><mi>d</mi><mi>l</mi><mo>.</mo><mi>B</mi></math> outwards

For equilibrium of element 

<math><mi mathvariant="italic">Idl B</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn><mi mathvariant="italic">T sin</mi><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="italic">d&#952;</mi></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn></mrow></mfrac></math>

For small angle <math><mi mathvariant="italic">sin&#952;</mi><mo>&#8771;</mo><mi>&#952;</mi></math>

<math><mi mathvariant="italic">Idl B</mi><mo>=</mo><mi>T</mi><mfrac><mrow><mi mathvariant="italic">dl</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>R</mi></mrow></mfrac></math>

### Q75. JEE Main 2014 (12 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Consider two thin identical conducting wires covered with very thin insulating material. One of the wires is bent into a loop and produces magnetic field $\mathrm{B}_1$, at its centre when a current $\mathrm{I}$ passes through it. The ratio $\mathrm{B}_1: \mathrm{B}_2$ is:

**Options:**

- **(A)** 1:1
- **(B)** $1: 3$
- **(C)** $1: 9$
- **(D)** $9: 1$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $1: 3$**

**Detailed Solution:**

For loop $B=\frac{\mu_0 \mathrm{nI}}{2 \mathrm{a}}$ where, $a$ is the radius of loop.
Then, $B_1=\frac{\mu_0 I}{2 a}$
Now, for coil $\mathrm{B}=\frac{\mu_0 \mathrm{I}}{4 \pi} \cdot \frac{2 \mathrm{nA}}{\mathrm{x}^3}$ at the centre $x=$ radius of loop
$$
\begin{aligned}
\mathrm{B}_2 &=\frac{\mu_0}{4 \pi} \cdot \frac{2 \times 3 \times(\mathrm{I} / 3) \times \pi(\mathrm{a} / 3)^2}{(\mathrm{a} / 3)^3} \\
&=\frac{\mu_0 \cdot 3 \mathrm{I}}{2 \mathrm{a}} \\
\therefore & \frac{\mathrm{B}_1}{\mathrm{~B}_2}=\frac{\mu_0 \mathrm{I} / 2 \mathrm{a}}{\mu_0 \cdot 3 \mathrm{I} / 2 \mathrm{a}} \\
\mathrm{B}_1: \mathrm{B}_2=1: 3
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q76. JEE Main 2014 (11 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Three straight parallel current carrying conductors are shown in the figure. The force experienced by the middle conductor of length $25 \mathrm{~cm}$ is:
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/UMiMxoSt4p1y6ocU1JOLWRTrB8XjcN_IB13s7I6aMXs.original.fullsize.png">

**Options:**

- **(A)** $3 \times 10^{-4} \mathrm{~N}$ toward right
- **(B)** $6 \times 10^{-4} \mathrm{~N}$ toward right
- **(C)** $9 \times 10^{-4} \mathrm{~N}$ toward right
- **(D)** Zero

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $3 \times 10^{-4} \mathrm{~N}$ toward right**

**Detailed Solution:**

$$
\text { } I_1=30 \mathrm{~A} \quad I=10 \mathrm{~A} \quad I_2=20 \mathrm{~A}
$$
<img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/ekrxDCWWTj00HKbk8p6eG5mtkqIOeuoq0BMd8wTd0rg.original.fullsize.png">

Also given; length of wire $Q$ $=25 \mathrm{~cm}=0.25 \mathrm{~m}$
Force on wire $\mathrm{Q}$ due to wire $\mathrm{R}$
$$
\begin{aligned}
&F_{Q R}=10^{-7} \times \frac{2 \times 20 \times 10}{0.05} \times 0.25 \\
&=20 \times 10^{-5} \mathrm{~N} \text { (Towards left) }
\end{aligned}
$$
Force on wire $\mathrm{Q}$ due to wire $\mathrm{P}$
$$
\begin{aligned}
&F_{Q P}=10^{-7} \times \frac{2 \times 30 \times 10}{0.03} \times 0.25 \\
&=50 \times 10^{-5} \mathrm{~N} \text { (Towards right) } \\
&\text { Hence, } F_{\text {net }}=F_{Q P}-F_{Q R} \\
&=50 \times 10^{-5} \mathrm{~N}-20 \times 10^{-5} \mathrm{~N} \\
&=3 \times 10^{-4} \mathrm{~N} \text { towards right }
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q77. JEE Main 2013 (22 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A current $i$ is flowing in a straight conductor of length $L$. The magnetic induction at a point on its axis at a distance $\frac{L}{4}$ from its centre will be :

**Options:**

- **(A)** Zero
- **(B)** $\frac{\mu_0 i}{2 \pi L}$
- **(C)** $\frac{\mu_0 i}{\sqrt{2} L}$
- **(D)** $\frac{4 \mu_0 i}{\sqrt{5} \pi L}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) Zero**

**Detailed Solution:**

Magnetic field at any point lies on axial position of current carrying conductor $\mathrm{B}=0$

</details>

### Q78. JEE Main 2013 (09 Apr Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

An electric current is flowing through a circular coil of radius $\mathrm{R}$. The ratio of the magnetic field at the centre of the coil and that at a distance $2 \sqrt{2} R$ from the centre of the coil and on its axis is :

**Options:**

- **(A)** $2 \sqrt{2}$
- **(B)** 27
- **(C)** 36
- **(D)** 8

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) 27**

**Detailed Solution:**

Given $:$ Radius $=R$
Distance $\mathrm{x}=2 \sqrt{2} \mathrm{R}$
$$
\begin{aligned}
\frac{B_{\text {centre }}}{B_{\text {axis }}} & =\left(1+\frac{x^2}{R^2}\right)^{3 / 2}=\left(1+\frac{(2 \sqrt{2} R)^2}{R^2}\right)^{3 / 2} \\
& =(9)^{3 / 2}=27
\end{aligned}
$$

</details>

### Q79. JEE Main 2012 (26 May Online)

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Currents of a 10 ampere and 2 ampere are passed through two parallel thin wires $A$ and $B$ respectively in opposite directions. Wire $A$ is infinitely long and the length of the wire $B$ is $2 \mathrm{~m}$. The force acting on the conductor $B$, which is situated at $10 \mathrm{~cm}$ distance from $A$ will be

**Options:**

- **(A)** $8 \times 10^{-5} \mathrm{~N}$
- **(B)** $5 \times 10^{-5} \mathrm{~N}$
- **(C)** $8 \pi \times 10^{-7} \mathrm{~N}$
- **(D)** $4 \pi \times 10^{-7} \mathrm{~N}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $8 \times 10^{-5} \mathrm{~N}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Force acting on conductor $B$ due to conductor $A$ is given by relation $F=\frac{\mu_0 I_1 I_2 l}{2 \pi r}$
$l$-length of conductor $B$
$r$-distance between two conductors
$$
\therefore F=\frac{4 \pi \times 10^{-7} \times 10 \times 2 \times 2}{2 \times \pi \times 0.1}=8 \times 10^{-5} \mathrm{~N}
$$

</details>

### Q80. JEE Main 2010

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

Two long parallel wires are at a distance $2 \mathrm{~d}$ apart. They carry steady equal current flowing out of the plane of the paper as shown. The variation of the magnetic field along the line $\mathrm{XX}$ ' is given by

**Options:**

- **(A)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/6j-hM7d63aSxrzPF1JWsyMGVEhUgEj3l-Ida0ehK0kA.original.fullsize.png">
- **(B)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/uSp5XGwZHqXNKZmYkLFYAZjHdWFPOKRwivH1uHOYpmc.original.fullsize.png">
- **(C)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/mF3JuQdGd1hIyezYaeYQ0y1MH20LeF7KsP_FNwwob0Y.original.fullsize.png">
- **(D)** <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/7sST8_gsLJFGrcopCtFM6Hu1NTnSTqOHmBdNNQWSVCw.original.fullsize.png">

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) <img src="https://cdn-question-pool.getmarks.app/pyq/jee_main/6j-hM7d63aSxrzPF1JWsyMGVEhUgEj3l-Ida0ehK0kA.original.fullsize.png">**

**Detailed Solution:**

The magnetic field in between because of each will be in opposite direction
$$
\begin{aligned}
B_{\text {in between }} & =\frac{\mu_0 i}{2 \pi x} \hat{j}-\frac{\mu_0 i}{2 \pi(2 d-x)}(-\hat{j}) \\
& =\frac{\mu_0 i}{2 \pi}\left[\frac{1}{x}-\frac{1}{2 d-x}\right](\hat{j})
\end{aligned}
$$
at $\quad x=d, \quad B_{\text {in between }}=0$
for $\quad x < d, \quad B_{\text {in between }}=(\hat{j})$
for $\quad x>d, \quad B_{\text {in between }}=(-\hat{j})$
towards $\mathrm{x}$ net magnetic field will add up and direction will be $(-\hat{\mathrm{j}})$
towards $\mathrm{x}^{\prime}$ net magnetic field will add up and direction will be $(\hat{\mathrm{j}})$

</details>

### Q81. JEE Main 2008

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A horizontal overhead power line is at a height of $4 \mathrm{~m}$ from the ground and carries a current of $100 \mathrm{~A}$ from east to west. The magnetic field directly below it on the ground is $\left(\mu_0=4 \pi \times 10^{-7} \mathrm{~T} \mathrm{~m} \mathrm{~A}^{-1}\right)$

**Options:**

- **(A)** $2.5 \times 10^{-7} \mathrm{~T}$ southward
- **(B)** $5 \times 10^{-6} \mathrm{~T}$ northward
- **(C)** $5 \times 10^{-6}$ T southward
- **(D)** $2.5 \times 10^{-7}$ northward

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $5 \times 10^{-6}$ T southward**

**Detailed Solution:**

$$
B=\frac{\mu_0}{2 \pi} \frac{i}{R}=\frac{4 \pi \times 10^{-7}}{2 \pi} \times \frac{100}{4}=5 \times 10^{-6} \mathrm{~T} \text { southward }
$$

</details>

### Q82. JEE Main 2007

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Two identical conducting wires $\mathrm{A O B}$ and $\mathrm{C O D}$ are placed at right angles to each other. The wire $\mathrm{AOB}$ carries an electric current $\mathrm{I_1}$ and $\mathrm{COD}$ carries a current $\mathrm{I_2}$. The magnetic field on a point lying at a distance ' $\mathrm{d}$ ' from $\mathrm{O}$, in a direction perpendicular to the plane of the wires $\mathrm{A O B}$ and $\mathrm{C O D}$, will be given by

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{\mu_0}{2 \pi}\left(\frac{\mathrm{I}_1+\mathrm{I}_2}{d}\right)^{1 / 2}$
- **(B)** $\frac{\mu_0}{2 \pi \mathrm{d}}\left(\mathrm{I}_1^2+\mathrm{I}_2^2\right)^{1 / 2}$
- **(C)** $\frac{\mu_0}{2 \pi d}\left(\mathrm{I}_1+\mathrm{I}_2\right)$
- **(D)** $\frac{\mu_0}{2 \pi d}\left(\mathrm{I}_1^2+\mathrm{I}_2^2\right)$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $\frac{\mu_0}{2 \pi}\left(\frac{\mathrm{I}_1+\mathrm{I}_2}{d}\right)^{1 / 2}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\frac{\mu_0 \mathrm{I}}{2 \pi \mathrm{d}} \sqrt{\left(\mathrm{I}_1^2+\mathrm{I}_2^2\right)}$

</details>

### Q83. JEE Main 2007

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A current $\mathrm{I}$ flows along the length of an infinitely long, straight, thin walled pipe. Then

**Options:**

- **(A)** the magnetic field is zero only on the axis of the pipe
- **(B)** the magnetic field is different at different points inside the pipe
- **(C)** the magnetic field at any point inside the pipe is zero
- **(D)** the magnetic field at all points inside the pipe is the same, but not zero

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) the magnetic field at any point inside the pipe is zero**

**Detailed Solution:**

Use Ampere's law

</details>

### Q84. JEE Main 2007

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A long straight wire of radius ' $a$ ' caries a steady current $\mathrm{i}$. The current is uniformly distributed across its cross section. The ratio of the magnetic field at $\frac{a}{2}$ and $2 a$ is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\frac{1}{4}$
- **(B)** $4$
- **(C)** $1$
- **(D)** $\frac{1}{2}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $1$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$\mathrm{B} 2 \pi \frac{\mathrm{a}}{2}=\mu_0 \frac{\mathrm{i}}{\pi \mathrm{a}^2}\left(\frac{\pi \mathrm{a}^2}{4}\right)$
$\mathrm{B}_1=\frac{\mu_0 \mathrm{i}}{4 \pi \mathrm{a}} \quad \ldots$ (i)
$\mathrm{B}_2 2 \pi(2 \mathrm{a})=\mu_0 \mathrm{i}$
$\mathrm{B}_2=\frac{\mu_0 \mathrm{i}}{4 \pi \mathrm{a}} \quad \dots (ii)$
$\frac{\mathrm{B}_1}{\mathrm{~B}_2}=1$

</details>

### Q85. JEE Main 2006

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

A long solenoid has 200 turns per $\mathrm{cm}$ and carries a current $\mathrm{i}$. The magnetic field at its centre is $6.28 \times 10^{-2}$ Weber $/ \mathrm{m}^2$. Another long solenoid has 100 turns per $\mathrm{cm}$ and it carries a current $\mathrm{i} / 3$. The value of the magnetic field at its centre is

**Options:**

- **(A)** $1.05 \times 10^{-4}$ Weber/m
- **(B)** $1.05 \times 10^{-2}$ Weber $/ \mathrm{m}^2$
- **(C)** $1.05 \times 10^{-5} \mathrm{Weber} / \mathrm{m}^2$
- **(D)** $1.05 \times 10^{-3} \mathrm{Weber} / \mathrm{m}^2$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $1.05 \times 10^{-2}$ Weber $/ \mathrm{m}^2$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$B_2=\frac{B_1 n_2 i_2}{n_1 i_1}=\frac{\left(6.28 \times 10^{-2}\right)(100 \times i / 3)}{200(i)}=1.05 \times 10^{-2} \mathrm{~W} / \mathrm{m}^2$

</details>

### Q86. JEE Main 2005

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

Two concentric coils each of radius equal to $2 \pi \mathrm{cm}$ are placed at right angles to each other. 3 Ampere and 4 ampere are the currents flowing in each coil respectively. The magnetic induction in Weber $/ \mathrm{m}^2$ at the centre of the coils will be $\left(\mu_0=4 \pi \times 10^{-7}\right.$ Wb/A-m)

**Options:**

- **(A)** $12 \times 10^{-5}$
- **(B)** $10^{-5}$
- **(C)** $5 \times 10^{-5}$
- **(D)** $7 \times 10^{-5}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(C) $5 \times 10^{-5}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$B=\frac{\mu_o}{2 r} \sqrt{I_1^2+I_2^2}$
$B=\frac{4 \pi \times 10^{-7}}{2 \times 2 \pi \times 10^{-2}} \times 5$
$B=5 \times 10^{-5}$

</details>

### Q87. JEE Main 2004

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `3`

The magnetic field due to a current carrying circular loop of radius $3 \mathrm{~cm}$ at a point on the axis at a distance of $4 \mathrm{~cm}$ from the centre is $54 \mu \mathrm{T}$. What will be its value at the centre of the Loop?

**Options:**

- **(A)** $250 \mu \mathrm{T}$
- **(B)** $150 \mu \mathrm{T}$
- **(C)** $125 \mu \mathrm{T}$
- **(D)** $75 \mu \mathrm{T}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) $250 \mu \mathrm{T}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

Using formula $B=\frac{\mu_0^{\text {II }}}{2\left(R^2+X^2\right)^{3 / 2}}$, we get
$$
54=\frac{\mu_0 i(3)^2}{2\left[(3)^2+(4)^2\right]^{3 / 2}}
$$
At the centre of the coil, $X=0$ and $B=\frac{\mu_0 i}{2(3)}$
Using equation (i)
$$
B=\frac{54 \times 5^3}{(3)^2 \times 3} \Rightarrow B=250 \mu \mathrm{T} \text {. }
$$

</details>

### Q88. JEE Main 2004

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A long wire carries a steady current. It is bent into a circle of one turn and the magnetic field at the centre of the coil is $B$. It is then bent into a circular loop of $n$ turns. The magnetic field at the centre of the coil will be

**Options:**

- **(A)** $\mathrm{nB}$
- **(B)** $\mathrm{n}^2 \mathrm{~B}$
- **(C)** $2 \mathrm{nB}$
- **(D)** $2 n^2 B$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) $\mathrm{n}^2 \mathrm{~B}$**

**Detailed Solution:**

$B^{\prime}=\frac{\pi \mu_0 I}{2 r^{\prime}}=n^2 \frac{\mu_0 \pi \pi}{\ell}=n^2 B$.

</details>

### Q89. JEE Main 2004

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `1`

A current I ampere flows along an infinitely long straight thin-walled tube, then the magnetic induction at any point inside the tube is

**Options:**

- **(A)** infinite
- **(B)** zero
- **(C)** $\frac{\mu_0}{4 \pi} \frac{2 \mathrm{i}}{r}$ tesla
- **(D)** $2 n^2 \mathrm{~B}$

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(B) zero**

**Detailed Solution:**

Considering Ampere's loop (shown by dotted line), no current is enclosed by this loop. Therefore, the magnetic field will be zero inside the tube.

</details>

### Q90. JEE Main 2002

*Type:* `singleCorrect` | *Level:* `2`

If in a circular coil $A$ of radius $R$, current I is flowing and in another coil $B$ of radius $2 R$ a current $2 \mathrm{l}$ is flowing, then the ratio of the magnetic fields $\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{A}}$ and $\mathrm{B}_{\mathrm{B}}$, produced by them will be

**Options:**

- **(A)** 1
- **(B)** 2
- **(C)** 1/2
- **(D)** 4

<details>
<summary><b>View Answer & Solution</b></summary>

**Correct Option:** **(A) 1**

**Detailed Solution:**

In coil A, B $=\frac{\mu_0}{4 \pi} \frac{2 \pi \mathrm{I}}{\mathrm{R}} . \quad \therefore \mathrm{B} \alpha \frac{\mathrm{I}}{\mathrm{R}}$; Hence, $\frac{\mathrm{B}_1}{\mathrm{~B}_2}=\frac{\mathrm{I}_1}{\mathrm{R}_1} \cdot \frac{\mathrm{R}_2}{\mathrm{I}_2}=\frac{2}{2}=1$

</details>
